# Changelog

All notable changes to Nexira (formerly Aura Launcher) will be
documented in this file. The format is based on
[Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/).

This file is the engineering log, and only that. Name the actual classes,
files and mechanism, and the reason behind the change, not the user-facing
feature (see 2.2.12 for the reference depth). Scale the depth to the release
weight: a fast beta can stay thin, a stable rollup carries the full writeup
consolidating its betas.

What a release means for the person using the launcher belongs in
[CHANGELOG_EN.md](./CHANGELOG_EN.md), with `CHANGELOG_RU.md`,
`CHANGELOG_DE.md` and `CHANGELOG_JA.md` as its translations, one per shipped
interface language and named by that language's own tag. That is the file the in-app update
dialog renders and the one a player reads, so it is written in a player's
terms and owes nothing to this one. The two are not two depths of the same
text: an entry can matter here and be invisible there, or the reverse. Same
version headers and dates in both, which is how an entry is matched across.

Author each bullet as one physical line, no manual line wrapping. A `.md`
renders the same either way, and these notes feed the GitHub release body
verbatim, where a hand-wrapped line would render as a <br> staircase.

## [Unreleased]

### Changed
- Qodana leaves out `DestructingShortFormNameMismatch` and `CustomComponentDestructuringMigration`, Kotlin 2.5's migration hints for name-based destructuring, which made 226 of the 255 problems a release scan reported. Every site destructures in the order its names say, and a language version that turns the compiler's own check on fails the build at all of them through `allWarningsAsErrors`. A `bootstrap` step produces the sources the analysis reads and the Gradle import does not, `Res` in client-ui and nx-ui, `BuildConfig` and the KSP widget registry, which the report listed as sanity problems. A bootstrap that fails does not fail the scan, since the comment the scan posts is what the release workflow's review gate reads. `KotlinUnreachableCode` and `KotlinConstantConditions` are back on: excluded while the 2025.2 linter misread ordinary control flow, the 2026.2 one finds nothing unreachable and two conditions that were indeed constant, a fallback to a value already known null in `ActivityPillWidget` and an effect key in `SkinView3D` that its enclosing `if` holds true. Both are gone. The remaining findings were read in the code: the ones that are not real are suppressed where they stand, the rest of the style hints are taken.

### Fixed
- A press on the edge of an `NxField` did not reach the field. Its inset was padding around the text field rather than inside it, so a press there landed on nothing and, to the shell, fell outside the focused field, which let go of the caret. The inset is now part of the field's decoration.
- The console lost its shortcuts after a press on its own chrome. Embedded in a pack's Logs tab, a press on the toolbar's text or the status line let the shell clear the focus, and the console reads Ctrl+F, F3 and the scroll keys only while something in it is focused. `holdFocusOnPress` gives the focus back to the part of the console that last held it, the log, the search or the command line. The search prompt's buttons no longer take the focus from the query, which sent the next letters typed to the log's shortcuts.
- Clearing Browse's search with its own button lost the caret. The press handed the focus to the button, the clear then removed the button, and the next keys went nowhere until the field was clicked again. The button now gives the caret back to the field.
- A category tag clicked on a project page could pull the reader into Browse after they had gone elsewhere. The search waits up to three seconds for the catalogue's lists on the shell's scope, which outlives the page, and then rewrote Browse's question and opened it whatever was on screen by then. It now does nothing when the screen changed while it waited.
- A project followed from a link on another project's page lost the pack that page installed into. A dependency row on a build's page, a link in a description and a catalogue link in the rail all opened the new page with no pack behind it, so it asked which pack to install into, or offered nothing for a file in a pack. The link follower now carries the pack of the page holding the link, and a modpack link still opens on its own pack page.
- Coming back to a project page named its crumb by the catalogue id until the page had loaded, though the trail already knew the project's name from the visit before. A page that has not heard back publishes the route's id as its title, and the crumb took it from there over the remembered name. The crumb now reads the open page's title only once the page is no longer pending.
- A mirror pack's page could stand a day behind the mirror, and its Install with it. The page read `details` once, and the summary, manifest and build listing caches hand an expired entry back at once and refresh behind the reader, so the page showed the stored runtime, sign-in and build list, and Install took the build that was newest when the copy was made. `IPackCatalogueService.detailsStream` gives the stored page and then the mirror's answer, `SmrtPackClient` streams the summary and the manifest the way it already streamed the build listing, and a refresh that fails keeps the stored read on screen.
- Going Back from a project page before its fade had finished left the rail wrong. The shell keeps the screen it returns to and does not run its effects a second time, so that screen never took the rail again: the page leaving cleared `OpenProjectState` and the rail beside a project page stood empty, and the family count only knew that someone still wanted a family, so Browse went on beside the project view. `OpenProjectState` and `SurfaceFamilies` now keep each claiming screen in order with what it last asked for, and a release hands the rail to the newest screen still mounted.
- The catalogue's search cache grew on disk without end. `ModrinthCaches.search` is keyed by the whole URL, query, filters, order, offset and the ids a search leaves out, so its keys hardly repeat, and while `maxEntries` bounds the memory half, the Xodus store behind it never removed an entry the cache would never serve again. `XodusDiskStore` takes an age (`maxDiskAgeMs` on `CacheFactory.create`) and sweeps what is older on the first write of a run and every 64 after it, reading the envelope's `stored_at` rather than decoding each page, and the search cache keeps a page on disk for the day it can be served offline.
- A build's own page installed it without a word either way. Its Install had no mark for a build the pack does not run, which the versions table marks in orange one click away, and nothing was said after the click: a refusal, a failed download, a dependency left out or a mod refused by a mirror pack all ended with the button back as it was. The page marks a build that does not fit, says what the install came to under the button with the project page's own notes (`InstallNotes`, shared by both), offers Retry after a failure and reads installed after a success. A build page opened on a file that has gone since, or that the catalogue does not know, waited forever for a build nobody would ask for and says so now.
- A project page's rail read "?" for the game versions whenever the catalogue's own version list could not be fetched, about a project that names its versions. The catalogue branch of `ModDetailState.publish` falls back to the project's versions unfolded, as the mirror branch and the pack page already did.
- A project page left from its Gallery tab came back on Description. The page's state is new on every visit and has no shots on its first frame, and the effect that moves off an empty gallery ran then; it waits for the page to load.
- Retry on a project page installed a different build from the one that failed. A row of the versions table installs the build it names, and when that failed the header's button turned to Retry and ran its own install, the newest build that fits. `ModDetailState.retryInstall` repeats the last install as it was asked for.
- Browse's list of mods, resource packs and shaders could read a pack wrong, or open in the wrong place. `ProjectBrowseState.loadPresent` marked the pack read before reading it, so a search cancelled during the read (typing on cancels the one before) left it unread for the rest of the screen: installed projects offered Install and hiding them hid nothing. A kept search came back with what the pack held when it was kept, a mod installed from a project page or removed in the Content tab since included; a stamp of the content folders' names, sizes and change times (`contentStampOf`) is kept with it now, and the pack is read again only when it differs. A screen left behind with an install still running wrote its shorter list back over the search the reader had come back to, and a snapshot older than the one in the session is no longer written. A new query opened at the previous one's scroll position, past the end of a shorter list, which paged on at once, and a position was recorded under the question asked next. The list is one per question shown (`shownQuery`), starts a new question at the top, and records its position under its own question.
- An install from Browse or the install dialog that left a required dependency out read as done. `problemOf` answers only whether the build asked for landed, and neither the card nor the dialog read `Outcome.missing`, so only the project page said the game would refuse the mod. The card (`ProjectBrowseState.missing`), the dialog's row (`RowState.Done.missing`) and its new-pack tab now name how many dependencies the install went without, with the build still counted as installed.
- A rollback gave each file back under the name it had when the snapshot was taken, while the record kept the choice as it is now, so a mod or a resource pack switched since came back on in the record and off in the game, or the other way round. This was there for mods before the assets joined it. `PackUpdateService.rollback` relabels the restored files from the restored build's manifest once the restore is done, and leaves the names as restored when that manifest cannot be read.
- A resource or shader pack switched off while the game ran stayed on after it. The game keeps a selected pack's zip open the way it keeps a jar, which on Windows refuses the rename, and `relabelAssets` only logged the refusal on the premise that the game does not hold these files, so the next launch loaded the pack while the record and the Content tab said off. A refused asset move is written to `PendingVariants` like a mod's, which now names files in `resourcepacks/` and `shaderpacks/` as well as `mods/`, keyed by the asset's path and still only ever between one file's own two names, and the next launch carries it out.
- Without the pack's manifest, offline or for a build the mirror has retired, the Content tab read every resource and shader pack a mirror pack ships as the player's own: a required one had a raw switch and a delete, and a catalogue update could be offered for one before the manifest arrived and stayed offered after it, so applying it put a second build beside the one the next sync would restore. The tab locks the assets the installed build names (`installedManifest`, read without the network) until the manifest says which may be switched, shows them as they lie on disk, and offers and applies an update only for a row the player may replace (`liveUpdates`).
- A switched-off resource or shader pack from CurseForge, GitHub or the mirror's own store came back on at the next build, and two from one project were one choice. `SmrtAssetEntry.stableKey` was the Modrinth project or else the path, and a path carries the version as often as a mod's file name does. It follows the mod key's chain now (a `slug` the manifest may carry, then the Modrinth, CurseForge or GitHub project, then the path), a choice kept under the path (`pathKey`) is still read, and `OptionalContentRules.assetKeys` gives each of two switchable assets that share a key its own path key, where switching either used to move both in the record while the disk followed only the last.
- Any file a mirror pack ships under `resourcepacks/` or `shaderpacks/` without the required flag was offered as a switch, a file of a pack shipped unpacked or OptiFine's `X.zip.txt` settings included, and switching one off renamed that single file, which broke the pack it belonged to. `SmrtAssetEntry.isPackArchive` takes only a `.zip` directly in either folder, and `toggleable`, the off-name handling in the sync and the instance scan all read it.
- Three kinds of work looked at whether a pack was free and marked it busy later, where `InstanceWorkRegistry.claim` does both in one step. The startup auto-update marked the pack only once its network check came back, so a Play or a catalogue install accepted in between ran beside the update, and a launch's roster sweep against the old baseline could delete the jars the update had just put in. `InstanceContentUpdater.start` marked the pack only when its job ran on another thread, and a catalogue install claiming it meanwhile had its download swept away by the batch's first step. A delete was marked a step after its check. All three claim the pack now, the auto-update and the delete ask about the running game again once the mark is up, and a batch cancelled before it ever ran lets its mark go.
- An install could put a mod in beside a dependency that would not load, or put a dependency in twice. `ContentInstaller.plan` counted a dependency as present when any file of its project was in the pack, a switched-off one included, so the mod went in beside a library no loader reads and the game failed naming it. And presence was only what the catalogue recognised by bytes, so a library already there as a CurseForge build or a file put in by hand got a second copy of the same mod id, which the loader refuses to start with. A dependency counts as present only when a file of it is on, one carried only switched off is named (`Skip.SwitchedOff`, reported among what is missing), and a downloaded dependency whose own mod id a file of the pack already provides is dropped before it is placed (`InstanceContentScanner.ownModIds`, the ids of nested jars left out so a mod that merely bundles a library is not taken for it).
- A modpack could be installed into a pack as a mod. A `modrinth.com/modpack/...` link opened the project page, whose install offered every pack that runs the modpack's loader, because the catalogue's v2 API lists a modpack build under its pack's loaders rather than `mrpack`, and `placementFor` read a Fabric modpack as a Fabric mod: its `.mrpack` index landed in `mods/`, which no loader reads and the Content tab never lists. `placementIn` refuses a build whose file is an `.mrpack` as `Modpack`, and a modpack link (`modrinthProjectLink`) opens the catalogue pack page.
- Browse could install a build for the game version a pack used to run. `rememberProjectBrowseState` kept its state by the pack's id, while the target is read off disk only after the screen has drawn once, so returning to a pack updated since to another version kept the previous visit's reading, and with the game version and loader unlocked nothing else rebuilt it: the install asked the catalogue for the old version and placed that build. The state is keyed on the whole destination now, what the pack runs included, and a pack that cannot be read drops the old reading instead of answering with it.
- A resource or shader pack the pack requires, found under its `.disabled` name, stayed off. `relabelAssets`, `planAsset` and the asset branch of `applyUpdate` handled only the assets a player may switch, so one switched off while it was optional and then made required by the next build kept its off name: the scan found its bytes there, the update planned nothing, and the Content tab drew it on and locked while the game did not list it. Every asset that can carry an off name (`SmrtAssetEntry.hasOffName`) is placed now, and a required one is moved back on without fetching it again.
- An optional pack asset with no choice recorded was read as on. A resource pack the player had switched off by hand while the pack still shipped it as plain content sits under its `.disabled` name with nothing written in `optionalContent`, and the first relabel (any optional mod switched), update or repair put it back in the game. `OptionalContentRules.assetState` and `carried` take where the asset sits on disk (`placedIn`) for an asset the record does not mention, every caller that holds the instance's folder passes it, and the next write of the record keeps that answer.
- The install dialog told Modrinth what every pack holds. To answer which packs a project could go into, `InstallTargets.forProject` read each pack through `InstalledIndex.read`, which posts the sha1 of every mod, resource pack and shader to `/v2/version_files`, so opening the dialog once sent every file of every pack, SmartyCraft and mirror packs included, uncached and again on every open. The question is about one project whose build list the dialog has already fetched, and every build publishes its files' sha1, so `InstalledIndex.readAgainst` matches each pack's files against that list on this machine and nothing about the packs leaves it. `read` keeps asking the catalogue, and only for the one pack an install goes into, as in 2.4.6.
- A pack mod the loader moved into `mods/<mcversion>/` that could not be read was taken for a stranger: `isRelocatedPackMod` answered no on a failed open, so `pruneForeignEntries` deleted the pack's own jar and the session guard reported it as foreign content. A read failure is what an antivirus scanning a freshly moved file looks like, the case `digestScan` already keeps apart from a mismatch. `relocation` answers `UNREADABLE` now, the file stays, and `enforceRoster` and `inspectRoster` name it under `unreadable`, which still leaves the instance unverified. The two read failures log apart, the relocated one with its path under `mods/`.
- `InstanceContentManager.replace` holds the new file's name to a bare file name, as `place` already did. It only kept the name inside the folder, so a catalogue answer naming `1.12.2/x.jar` would have put an update into `mods/1.12.2/`, which Forge also reads, beside the build it replaced. Its refusal names the file it was replacing (`Refusing to replace`), where it logged the line `place` logs.

## [2.4.7-beta] - 2026-10-09

### Added
- `ContentInstaller` puts a catalogue build into a pack as one planned piece of work. `plan` reads the pack through `InstalledIndex`, walks the required dependencies three levels down, takes a pinned build over the newest, and names every project it leaves out with a `Skip` reason: present, already installed, no build, lookup failed, no place, not taken by this pack, name taken, failed, not attempted, too deep, owned by the pack. A required dependency past the third level is named `TooDeep` and reported missing rather than dropped, and a build whose file to replace is one the pack's `PackFileRecord` names is refused as `PackOwned`, because a pack update would put the pack's own file back beside the replacement. `install` refuses while the pack's game runs or other `InstanceWork` rewrites it, takes its mark through `InstanceWorkRegistry.claim` and asks the running check again once marked, the order `PackInstanceService.deleteCompletely` uses, and queues a second install on the same pack behind the first rather than racing it for a name. Each file is downloaded to a `.nexira-update-*.part` scratch beside its folder, held to the published sha1 by the transfer, and moved in by `InstanceContentManager.replace` or the new `place`, which refuses a name already on disk in either state and a name that is not a bare file name. When the build that was asked for does not land, nothing behind it is tried. A folder that cannot take a download fails that step alone and keeps what already landed in the answer, and a dependency one branch of the walk named and another landed is not reported left out. `ModInstaller` keeps its signatures and its `Outcome` and finds the pack by its folder, and `Outcome.headLeftOut` carries why the build asked for was left out when the install could say why, which the project page, a card and the install dialog show instead of a retry.
- `InstalledIndex` reads what an instance holds by catalogue project across `mods/`, `resourcepacks/` and `shaderpacks/`, hashing through the `file-sha1` cache of `ModIconLookups`, which is now a Koin single of its own. It writes nothing into the instance, because the snapshots, the foreign importer and the roster sweep all reason about what is inside it. A reading is incomplete when a folder could not be listed, an archive's metadata did not parse (`InstanceContentScanner.scanReport` names what the scan skipped, where `scan` only logs it), a file could not be hashed or the catalogue could not be asked, and an install or `InstallTargets` treats any incomplete reading as a folder nobody could identify, so a file it could not see never gets a second build beside it.
- `InstanceWorkRegistry.claim` checks for other work and marks the instance under one lock, answering `Held` with a `Mark` to release or `Taken` with the work that holds it, and names the work an install may share the instance with.
- `ModrinthPlacement.kt` decides where a build goes from the loaders it is published for, after the catalogue's own loader table: a mod for a loader the pack runs goes to `mods/`, a mod that also ships as a data pack included, `minecraft` to `resourcepacks/`, `iris` and `optifine` to `shaderpacks/`, and `canvas` and `vanilla` to `resourcepacks/`, where the game and Canvas read those shaders. A data pack alone, a modpack, a server plugin and a mod for another loader are refused with a `PlacementRefusal`. `acceptedLoaders` says whose mods a pack runs: Quilt takes Fabric builds, NeoForge on 1.20.1 takes Forge builds, Cleanroom and lwjgl3ify take Forge builds, and a pack with no loader takes none.
- `InstanceWork.ContentInstall`, which the launch control names with its own label in all four languages and the download icon.
- Browse searches mods, resource packs and shaders beside packs, and is a widget surface now. The `browse` surface carries `browse.installing` (the pack being installed into), `browse.search` and `browse.chips` (the filters in force, each removable, the pack's own shown locked) in its `header` slot and `browse.results` in `body`. The right rail gains a third family, `browse` (`RailFamily.BROWSE`), whose `controls` slot carries `browse.scope` (the kind as four tiles, and for packs the source as a sliding switch) and one widget per filter after the catalogue's own sidebar: `browse.filter.installed`, `.version`, `.loader`, `.categories`, `.features`, `.resolutions`, `.performance`, `.environment`, `.license` and `.exclusions`. A filter block folds and unfolds, shows what it holds as chips while folded, and draws nothing for a kind it does not apply to. All of them meet at `BrowseController`, a Koin single holding the kind, source, query, order, the filters chosen per catalogue project type and the target pack, and the editor stands in for the screen with `STUB_BROWSE`. `BrowseScreen` switches the rail to the family while it is up and reads the chosen pack off disk into the controller.
- `SearchFilter`, `FilterField` and `searchExpression` in `modrinth/SearchFilters.kt` build the catalogue's own filter expression, sent as `new_filters` on the v2 search: game versions and loaders are any of them, categories all of them, a resolution any of them, an open licence a boolean, the environment the catalogue's lists of client and server values, and every excluded value one `NOT IN` per index field, disclosures and already installed projects included. `ModrinthClient.search` takes it, `searchProjects` narrows to a pack through it, and `categoryTags` and `loaderTags` read `/v2/tag/category` and `/v2/tag/loader`, which `BrowseTags` holds for the run. A category's SVG mark is drawn through `decodeToSvgPainter` in the row's ink.
- With a pack chosen, the game version and, for mods, the loaders the pack runs are locked filters (`BrowseController.lockedFor`) that can be unlocked per field and put back, unlocks being forgotten when the target changes, and `Hide installed` leaves out by id what the pack's folders already hold. What a search hides is fixed when it begins, so a project installed from the list stays on it reading installed and the pages after it keep their offsets. At most 200 ids go into the query, the rest are dropped on the client, and a page emptied that way is read past rather than taken as the end. A search is kept for the session (`ProjectBrowseSession`, keyed by type, question, order, filters, pack and whether installed projects are hidden), so coming back to it from a project page, or flipping to another kind and back, shows it as it was left, scroll position included, and asks nothing of the catalogue or of the pack for five minutes. Under it `ModrinthClient.search` and `searchModpacks` keep their pages by URL (`ModrinthCaches.search`, five minutes, the last page served offline for a day).
- A project result is a `ProjectCard` laid out after the catalogue's own list: the icon at 100dp, 64 below an 850dp column, the name at 20sp with its author, two lines of summary, the catalogue's display categories with the loaders drawn by `LoaderGlyph`, and the downloads, followers and last update down the right edge. `ModrinthSearchHit` decodes `author`, `downloads`, `follows`, `date_modified`, `date_created` and `display_categories` for it. Modrinth's modpacks are listed through the same search and cards, with their filters, and open the pack page. The card's install is one outlined control that reads install, installing, installed or retry, and the card says why under its summary when an install did not land. With a pack chosen that takes the kind it installs straight into it, and otherwise it opens `InstallDialog`.
- `InstallDialog`, the install for a project with no pack behind it, on a card and on the project page (`InstallChooser`, replacing `InstallPicker`). It names the project, lists every pack with its answer from `InstallTargets` (which now takes a build list the caller already holds) behind a search and a switch that hides the packs that do not fit, installs into the one picked with its state moving in the row, and counts the packs that fit. A second tab makes a new pack for the project with a loader and a game version its builds support and installs into it once `PackInstallService` has made it.
- `Screen.BrowseInto` is the same screen opened from inside a pack: the pack becomes the target on every entry, and each time the reader asks for it from the pack (`BrowseController.aimAt`) the search moves to a kind the pack takes (`kindsFor`, after `takesFromPlayer` and `acceptedLoaders`), so a mirror pack lands on resource packs and a pack with no loader is never offered mods to install. The header's back arrow returns to the pack and its cross stops installing into it. The breadcrumb reads Library, the pack, Find projects.
- `NxChoiceMenu` and `NxChoiceItem` in nx-ui, a list of answers to one question laid out after the catalogue's own listbox: full-width rows 44dp tall, the answer in force filled with the accent and named in it with a check, the row under the pointer or the keyboard lifted, hanging 8dp off its trigger at least as wide as it. `NxChoiceDivider` separates groups and `NxChoiceFooterItem` holds a row that changes what the list contains.
- A project's tags open the catalogue, the way the catalogue's own pages do: a category, a loader, a span of game versions or where it runs, in the project view's rail, the page header and a build's page, opens Browse on that tag alone for the project's type, nothing typed, with the page's pack as the target and the field the tag asks about unlocked from it. `ProjectTag`, `filtersFor` and `LocalCatalogueSearch` in `browse/CatalogueSearch.kt` and `BrowseController.searchFor` carry it. A category filed under resolutions searches as a resolution, which the search learns from the catalogue's lists and waits up to three seconds for (`BrowseTags.await`), and a folded version chip searches as every version behind it. A tag Browse cannot search by, such as a resource pack's `minecraft` loader, stays a label, and the rail offers a resource pack no loader block, since the catalogue keeps loaders and resolutions in one index field and the two chosen together were one either-of that ignored the resolution. `OpenProject` carries the project type, the page's pack and the version groups for it, and a clickable `NxPill` underlines under the pointer and gives under the press.
- The chips under the search fold the chosen game versions the way a project page folds them (`chosenChips`): a whole minor line reads `1.20.x` and a run inside one `1.20.2-1.20.4`, each taking all of its versions off at once, and clearing all is offered by chip count. A version the fold has no place for keeps a chip of its own.
- A catalogue pack's page stands on the project page's parts, for the mirror and Modrinth alike: `ProjectHeader` (the mark, the name, the line, the counts and tags, a labelled Install and the overflow), the same tabs, `BuildsTable` with an install on every build, `ChangelogList`, and the gallery with the mirror's banner at its head. It publishes an `OpenProject` and switches the right rail to the project view, so a pack shows compatibility, links, tags, team and details the way a mod does, and a Modrinth pack's tags open a search of packs. `OpenProject` carries a pack's runtime, the sign-in it asks for and its mod count, which the details block names, and leaves out a licence or a first release where the source has no such field. A page that could not be read takes the rail's facts down rather than leaving them pending, and its crumb reads the title the page published or the session last read (`catalogueTargetKey`) instead of asking the catalogue for the whole page again.
- `CataloguePackDetails` and `CataloguePackVersion` carry what both sources can say about a pack and its builds in the catalogue's terms: counts, game versions, loaders, environment, licence, dates, links, team, the build an install reaches for, the sign-in, and per build its downloads, mod count and size. A Modrinth pack build names no size, since its file is the `.mrpack` index of downloads.
- A file in a pack that the catalogue does not know gets its page from the mirror's registry (`GET /v1/mods/sha1:<hash>`, `SmrtPackClient.modByFile`) before falling back to what the jar declares: its name, who publishes it (CurseForge, or the mirror itself), a link to its project at that host, its authors, what it requires, which of the mirror's packs ship it in their current build (the registry's own answer names the newest build it ever saw the mod in, and its build index keeps links a rebuild removed), and its releases with their files in the versions table. `ProjectSource.Mirror` names such a page, `MirrorMod` reads the registry's answer, and the header falls back to the initial where the jar carries no icon. The jar's sha1 goes to the mirror only after the catalogue has not recognised it, and only for a mod jar, since the registry indexes nothing else, and a mirror that cannot be asked leaves the page as it was.
- The mirror's community packs are listed in Browse beside its own, from `GET /v1/community` (`SmrtPackClient.listCommunity` and `communityStream`, kept in the `pack-community` cache, which stores an empty listing since a mirror with no community packs yet is the ordinary case). `MirrorPackCatalogue` puts the mirror's own packs first in its order and the community's after them, a pack in both counting as the mirror's own, and ranks them no other way, since ranking by what players do would need the mirror to be told what they do. The community half joins the stream when it arrives and a failure there leaves the official packs listed. `CataloguePack.community` and `author` carry the owner's login to a byline on `BrowsePackCard`, and a community pack's page credits its owner in the rail's team block by name, without an avatar, since the only picture of a member is the GitHub one and asking GitHub for it would tell GitHub who looked. `browse.filter.builtBy` in the rail's browse family lists the mirror's own packs, its community's, or both (`BrowseController.mirrorOwn`, `mirrorCommunity`, `shows`) as two tiles switched on and off, the kind tiles' own `KindTile` with the mark filling in while on (`glyphFill`), and is there only while packs are listed from the mirror. The `Groups` and `Verified` glyphs join the bundled icon font for it.
- `RailBlock`, `RailGroup` and `RailLabel` hold the measurements of a rail card's inside, shared by the project view and the browse family. The `Block` and `LockOpen` glyphs join the bundled icon font.

### Changed
- The catalogue is one feature package, `hivens.ui.feature.catalogue`: `browse` holds what `screens/browse` and `widgets/browse` held, and `project` what `screens/mod` and `widgets/mod` held, tests alongside. The two pairs imported each other both ways, as every screen and its widgets do, so they were one thing filed under two layers. Grouped by feature they are what a later `client-ui` split can move as a whole.
- The project page and a catalogue pack's page are widget surfaces, `project` and `catalogue.pack`, each with a `header` slot (`project.header` and `project.tabs`, `catalogue.pack.header` and `catalogue.pack.tabs`) and a `body` slot (`project.body`, `catalogue.pack.body`), drawn on the two panels the pages always had. The widgets read the page they are drawn for through `LocalProjectPage` and `LocalCataloguePackPage`, a context per visit rather than a Koin single, because two pages are on screen at once while the shell fades between them, and draw nothing anywhere else. Both surfaces are in the editor, mounted on their screens, and a saved layout gets them seeded whole.
- A resource or shader pack a mirror pack ships answers to the pack by the `required` flag its manifest already carries (`SmrtAssetEntry.toggleable`, only under `resourcepacks/` and `shaderpacks/`). A required one is on and is not switched, deleted or replaced from the Content tab (`contentRowRules(assetEntry)`, `lockedCount`) or by the catalogue installer, which reads a mirror pack's own files from its installed build (`ContentDestination.Pack.packFiles`) and answers `Skip.PackOwned`. An optional one is switched through the pack like an optional mod: its choice is kept in `optionalContent` under `SmrtAssetEntry.stableKey` (`asset:` plus the Modrinth project, else the path), `OptionalContentRules.assetState`, `togglesFrom(manifest, mods, assets)` and `carried` keep both halves in every writer, `SmrtSyncService` places it under the name the choice gives it in `sync`, `verifyAndRepair` and `applyUpdate` (moving the pack's bytes across rather than fetching them again, and dropping a retired build's `.disabled` copy), `relabelAssets` moves it on a switch, and `scanInstanceState` and `managedRealPaths` see its `.disabled` name. Before this the Content tab let any of them be switched off, deleted or replaced, and the next update, repair or launch catch-up fetched the pack's file back by its path: a switched-off one came back beside its `.disabled` copy, a replaced one left two builds. Pack settings list the optional assets beside the optional mods.
- A plain primary press in the shell's content outside the element holding the keyboard focus releases it (`releaseFocusOnPress`, read on the first pass and never consumed), so a field gives up its caret and its accent when the reader clicks elsewhere. The node asks a focus target of its own that never takes the focus (`Focusability.Never`) where the focus is at the moment of the press (`getFocusedRect`), since `onFocusedBoundsChanged` is a no-op in this Compose. A press inside the focused element leaves it, so the version picker keeps its Escape and a slider its arrow keys after a click inside them, and so does a right press or a shift press, since those act on a field's own selection. The browse search field carries its clear and sort controls beside the text field rather than inside it, so the pointer on the sort control no longer lights the field and a press on it does not hand the field the caret.
- `ModrinthClient.newestMatchingVersion` delegates to `chooseBuild`: the newest fitting build by publish date on the stablest channel that has one, rather than the first build in the listing whose loaders contain the pack's exact loader name. A blank loader now means a pack that runs no mods, where it used to accept a build for any loader.
- What an install puts into a pack depends on where the pack came from (`takesFromPlayer`). A local pack takes anything. A mirror pack takes resource and shader packs and refuses mods, which `pruneForeignEntries` and the bound-launch `enforceRoster` would delete. Any other tracked pack takes files only while it keeps a `PackFileRecord`, and the install never writes to that record, so a pack update leaves the player's files alone.
- `loadersFor` asks about a mod under every loader `acceptedLoaders` gives for the pack and its game version, and about a resource pack under `canvas` and `vanilla` as well as `minecraft`, so a shader kept in `resourcepacks/` is checked for updates too.
- The Content tab's Find projects opens `Screen.BrowseInto` for the pack instead of the browser it carried inline. `ModBrowserState` and the inline `ModBrowser` are gone, their state became `ProjectBrowseState`, which takes the search, the install and the present-projects read as functions so its tests need no network. The button is offered on a mirror pack too, since a mirror pack now takes the player's resource packs and shaders.
- A project page names what an install left out and why, one line per project up to three (`ModDetailState.installLeftOut`, built from `ModInstaller.Outcome.skips` with the catalogue's titles), and why an install did not run at all (`installRefusal`).
- A widget that draws nothing takes no gap in a flow slot, so a block with nothing to show for the moment, a filter for another kind or a project block for a project that declares nothing, no longer leaves a double gap where it stands or a band of nothing under a column of them. In `SlotRenderer`'s unwrapped lines each widget that draws carries the gap before itself (`gapBefore`) and the line hands back the one before its first (`withoutLeadingGap`), so the measured line holds exactly the gaps it shows. An arrangement could only move the widgets inside a size the line had already measured with a gap for each.
- The catalogue's mod, resource pack and shader categories are named in all four languages, where most of them read as the raw catalogue word.
- `NxSelect` draws its list with `NxChoiceItem` rows and its trigger as a field with the answer in semibold, an optional muted `prefix` naming the question, and a chevron at the far edge that turns over while open. It takes a `hint` per answer and a `footer`. The browse sort, the language in settings, the log picker on the pack page, the game version and loader version lists in both create-pack flows and the game version in the install dialog moved onto these lists, the last of them onto `NxSelect` itself, and `NxMenuMark.Radio` with its drawn `RadioMark` is gone. The theme row in settings trails a chevron instead of a dropdown arrow, since it opens a screen.
- The catalogue pack page's banner hero, its unlabelled install glyph and the version picker it opened are gone: the header installs the build the source names as current, and the Versions tab installs any other. `CatalogueHero` and `compatFacts` went with them, their facts now in the rail.
- The project page's versions table marks a build as running on the pack by every loader the pack has a place for (`ModDetailState.packLoaders` over all three content kinds), so a resource pack is no longer marked as not running on a NeoForge pack. A per-row install is offered only where the page has a pack.

### Fixed
- The mirror's build listing names `minecraft_version`, `loader` and `size_bytes`, which `PackBuild` looked for under other names, so every mirror build decoded as running on nothing in particular and the versions screen could not grade one.
- The project view's rail said "?", "No licence stated" and "Publication date unknown" for the seconds the page was still loading, because the page handed it the project only once the team and the declarations had arrived too. The page publishes as soon as the project or the file is read, and before that `OpenProject.pending` draws an ellipsis in place of an answer.
- The versions table cut every build name at 180dp while half the panel stood empty beside it. The name column takes what the fixed columns leave, up to 400dp.
- A catalogue pack page wrote its loader with the first letter capitalised, "Neoforge", in its facts and in the version picker, and showed its tags as raw ids. Both go through `loaderLabel` and `modrinthCategory` now.
- A Library card for a pack with no version of its own carried a "—" chip. It carries none.
- The log picker on the pack page lost its hover while its list was open, the same as the other menu triggers.
- A control whose menu was open went dark when the menu closed and lit again a moment later under a pointer that never moved: the popup takes the pointer while it is up, and the hover only came back once its exit had finished. `NxMenuPopup` reports whether any of the popup is on screen (`onShownChange` on `NxChoiceMenu` and `NxContextMenu`), and `NxSelect`, the browse sort control and the language row (`NxRow.highlighted`) stay lit through it.
- A project page left behind under another screen showed its raw catalogue id in the breadcrumb, because the crumb read its name only from the page currently open. `OpenProjectState.names` keeps the names of the pages visited lately.
- A project page whose catalogue entry could not be fetched published itself to the rail as a file declaring nothing, so the rail beside the retry stated "?" and "no licence stated" about a project the page never read. It takes the rail's facts down, unless a file on disk still has its own.
- The project page's header and the rail's tags block printed the catalogue's raw category slugs. They use the translated names Browse already shows.
- `ModInstaller` wrote every build into `mods/`, a resource pack or a shader included, held no `InstanceMutationLock` and marked no `InstanceWork`, so Play could start a game over a half-installed mod and an install could change the folder of a running game. Another build of a project the pack already had went in beside the old file instead of replacing it, and a file name already in the folder counted as installed without its bytes being read. A dependency counted as present only when it sat in `mods/`. When the catalogue could not identify the folder, every dependency read as absent and was fetched again beside the copy already there: such a folder now gets nothing added.
- `InstanceContentUpdater.start` refuses while other work rewrites the instance, because a batch begins by sweeping the scratch files an install stages in the same folders.
- `SmrtPackClient` encodes a pack id as one path segment (`packUrl`) in every `/v1/packs/{id}/...` read and in a build's version. A community pack's id is namespaced by its owner, `u/<uid>/<name>`, and its slashes taken as separators asked the mirror for a route it does not have, so its page, its builds and its install all answered 404.
- An update or a repair started from the pack's window asked nothing of `InstanceWorkRegistry`, so it could run while a catalogue install was putting files into the same instance. `PackOperationService.start` claims the instance when it accepts the operation, before its job runs, and refuses when other work holds it, publishing `PackOperationPhase.Refused` with that work so the settings sheet and the versions screen say why nothing started.
- The shell fades between pages, so a project or pack page being left is still mounted and still loading after the arriving page has filled the right rail, and its late answers replaced the arriving page's facts. `OpenProjectState` takes facts only from the page that last claimed it (`claim`, `publish`, `release`), compared by identity.
- The pack page's hero chip wrote the loader by capitalising its first letter, so NeoForge read "Neoforge". It uses `loaderLabel` now, the table the project pages already use.

## [2.4.6] - 2026-10-07

### Added
- `appshell.topbar.sessions`, the running games as a title-bar widget: one compact entry per game with the pack name, the uptime and the console and stop actions, nothing while no game runs. It replaces `ActiveSessionsSection`, which was never mounted. Available from the palette, not placed by default.
- What the retired SmartyCraft server path left under `clients/` is now named and offered rather than left silent. `RetiredClientScanner` answers two separate questions: whether anything is there, which costs one directory listing and runs at every start, and what exactly is there, which walks gigabytes and runs once when somebody opens the surface. A sticky notice carries the first; `RetiredClientsHost` shows the second as one row per folder with its size, its mod count, and the Minecraft version and loader read out of the tree.
- The version and loader are read from `bin/natives-<mc>/`, then `assets-<mc>.zip`, then `libraries-<mc>/`, and the loader from the jars the client would boot through rather than from a log file. They are shown as editable rather than acted on, because a real install disagrees with itself: one client carries a Fabric loader log beside a Forge-only mod, and another's `bin/` and `lib/` are a bundled Windows JRE rather than game natives, which is most of why that one is two gigabytes.
- `RetiredClientAdopter` turns one folder into a `PackOrigin.Local` pack, and never a mirror one: a mirror pack is held to a manifest and a roster, and an adopted tree has neither, so calling it one would be a claim nothing could check. Content is hardlinked rather than copied -- nothing else owns `clients/` any more, so there is no second side to protect, and a copy would ask a disk that may not have it for the same gigabytes twice. The vanilla `assets/` tree is hardlinked into the shared root, so the provisioner's later pass finds it present and downloads nothing. A symbolic link pointing out of the tree is recreated as a link and one pointing into it is refused, since the sweep is about to remove its target. A file already at the destination counts as a failure, because within one adoption the walk yields each path once. A failed adoption removes its reserved instance directory, and the destructive pass runs on the app scope rather than the composition's.
- `RetiredDataSweeper` removes only what it is told to, and takes the bookkeeping (`manifest-cache/`, `profiles.json`, `servers-cache.json`) with the last client rather than the first, since those describe the set. Adoption runs before any sweep and an adoption that could not place every file keeps its source and says so on the row: the content is hardlinked, so removing the source of an incomplete transfer would turn a gap that could still be fixed into one that cannot. The default skins are extracted and cached once before the first deletion, because a client jar under `clients/` is the only one an upgraded install has until a pack is there. `RetiredDataSweeper.sweep` reports a tree it could only partly remove in `Swept.partial` instead of as untouched, subtracts the hardlinked bytes (`sharedBytesOf`) from the space it reports, and leaves `clients/` alone when the directory cannot be listed instead of reading it as empty. The default-skin cache counts as a hit with the two skins a pre-1.19.4 client carries.
- Ten player kinds replace the single music card, and the concept sheet is the specification rather than a sketch of one. `home.new.player.{cover,seeded,readout,timeline,tile,token,wave,ground,record,column}`, each a shape with its own argument: a cover-led card, one whose body borrows the artwork's colour through `seedFromImage`, a readout that never shows a picture because its corpus has none, a card whose own plane is the measure, a tile that is nothing but artwork until a pointer arrives, a token the size of a grid cell, and four built on the envelope. Layout migration step 9 re-points `home.new.music` at `home.new.player.cover` with props reset, since the retired card's only prop was a heading the successor takes from the file. Every kind binds to `MusicPlayerService`, which `PlaybackRouter` answers from the `AudioPlayer` or, while linked, from the wallpaper, so two on one surface are two views of one playback rather than two players competing for a device. The record and the token seek by the angle of a press or drag round the ring, clockwise from twelve, and leave presses inside the label to the transport, and the column's strip takes a drag. The tile, record, token and column are capped through `Modifier.playerObject` at a per-kind size prop: stretched to a home slot, a square token was seventeen hundred points across.
- `client-ui/.../audio/Waveform.kt` measures a track's envelope by decoding it, which needed no new library. `computeWaveform` hands an `EnvelopeSink` to a `VideoPlayer` and reads the file end to end at decode speed: the pacing in that pipeline is the sink's own blocking write, so a sink that never blocks never paces, and the cost is flat at about a second a file rather than proportional to its length, nearly all of it the player's bounded teardown. `PeakFold` folds interleaved PCM into one peak per 1024-frame window. It reads each sample at the encoding the stream opens with (U8, S16LE, S32LE, F32LE or F64LE) and normalises it to that encoding's range, because skinema decodes most music to F32LE and a float read as a short drew a flat line for every ordinary track. `resampleTo` reduces to 512 buckets by the max of each span rather than the mean, because averaging turns an outline into a loudness curve and loses the transients a track is recognised by. `WaveformCache` keys by path plus mtime plus length, since a path is not a version. `WaveformStrip`, `WaveformColumn` and `WaveformRing` draw it along, down and around, and the bar count comes from the room on screen rather than from the envelope, which carries more buckets than any card is wide.
- `MediaSessionBridge` publishes what the launcher is playing to the desktop and takes its media keys back, through `libsound-session`. MPRIS on Linux and the platform's own elsewhere, null-tolerant at every step: no session bus or a name another process holds means the launcher plays exactly as before and the desktop shows nothing. A cover is written to a new file name per track, because MPRIS carries a URL and a widget that already loaded one has no reason to read it again. The decisions live in `SessionPublisher` and `CoverTrail` over plain data, so `MediaSessionPublishTest` drives them with a recording session and no bus. The first poll is no longer announced as a seek, because the previous position starts absent instead of at zero.
- `SystemAudioOutput` opens the launcher's sound under its own name through `libsound-audio`, one backend for the process and a `SinkConfig` per track, wrapped by an adapter onto skinema's `PcmSink`. Without it skinema opens a line through JavaSound and the desktop's mixer shows an anonymous row labelled with the JVM's process name, with a volume slider belonging to the whole virtual machine rather than to the music. A machine it cannot reach falls back to that line, so this is an improvement to how the sound is labelled and never a condition on it playing. The stream names the `nexira` icon the desktop entry installs rather than `audio-x-generic`, so the mixer row is not the note glyph every other player draws. It is bound under the `AudioOutput` interface the player resolves and read with `getOrNull`, so a missing output cannot fail the eager `MediaSessionBridge` and the boot with it.
- The player's queue and its place in it survive a restart through `SettingsData.audioQueue` and `audioQueueIndex`, debounced at 800ms, and restoring opens nothing: the engine is what costs a decode thread and a device, and `play` already opens on demand.
- Legacy Fabric as a loader, under the id `legacy-fabric`, the tag Modrinth files its mods under. It is a third `FabricLikeResolver` over `meta.legacyfabric.net`, whose profiles have Fabric's shape. Its profile replaces LWJGL 2 and ships the natives as an old-style entry with a `natives` map and no plain jar, which the resolver took as a classpath library. Such an entry now becomes the loader's `nativesOverride`, one spec per platform classifier, and vanilla's natives for the same artifact are dropped through `removeFromBase`. The vanilla-launcher and Prism importers read the Fabric loader over Legacy Fabric's intermediary as Legacy Fabric. LiteLoader joins it as `LiteLoaderResolver`, which reads LiteLoader's index and starts the game through launchwrapper with its tweaker. Every fetch is made over https although the index writes http. A snapshot jar is found under its timestamped name through `maven-metadata.xml` and held to the sha1 its repository publishes beside it, and a release in the ivy repository is read from its own entry rather than "latest", whose file name is not served, and held to the index's md5 through a new md5 on `LibrarySpec`. Beside Forge it stays a mod in `mods/`, which FML loads through its TweakClass manifest entry. Legacy Fabric's natives jars carry every architecture, so an arm64 host with no `-arm64` build on offer takes the plain one for its OS. `EnvironmentPreparer` writes the jars a natives folder was built from beside it and rebuilds the folder when they differ, so a pack switched between loaders does not launch against the other loader's natives. A named LiteLoader build's resolved profile is kept through `LoaderSourceCache`, so a relaunch needs no network, and a build the index does not list is refused by name. A sha1 sidecar that cannot be fetched for any reason but its absence fails the resolve, since a profile kept with no digest would leave that build unverified for good. launchwrapper comes from Mojang's library host and the other libraries from Maven Central. A Prism instance whose only loader is LiteLoader is imported as a LiteLoader pack.
- A local pack's loader and loader version can be changed after creation. The Version section of a local pack's settings, hidden while there were no builds to manage, shows `PackLoaderSection`: the recorded loader and the create dialog's choices, which moved into a shared `LoaderPicker`. A change is a draft until applied, and applying writes `cachedManifest` only. The next launch installs the loader the way a pack left on the latest settles its version, and the Java still comes from the resolved runtime.
- The mirror's per-language text reaches the reader. `SmrtPackSummary` decodes `tagline_i18n` and `description_md_i18n` and `PackBuild` decodes `changelog_i18n`, all optional. `inLanguage` takes the reader's tag and falls back to the untagged copy, which is the whole rule because the mirror settles blank entries and tag case before shipping. `MirrorPackCatalogue` and `PackUpdateService` resolve them where the wire shape becomes the catalogue's, asking the settings for the language on every read, so no render site changes.
- A mod whose dependency is missing or too old is named before the game starts. `InstanceContentScanner` reads what each archive provides and requires: a mods.toml or neoforge.mods.toml table by table (`[[mods]]` ids with versions, a template header with a trailing comment and a byte-order mark handled, the jarVersion placeholder resolved, and hard `[[dependencies.<x>]]` entries with their ranges, optional and server-side ones left out, multi-line strings skipped), fabric.mod.json ids, provides and depends predicates (none for a server-only mod), quilt.mod.json ids, provides and unconditional non-optional depends, and what jars nested under `META-INF/jars` and `META-INF/jarjar` provide, including for a jar whose only mod is nested. Requirements are tagged with the manifest they came from, and `dependencyIssues` reads only the manifests the pack's loader reads (`manifestsFor`). It reports, on the mod that needs it, a requirement nothing enabled provides and one whose providers are all outside its range. `VersionRanges` reads Maven ranges as Forge does and Fabric predicates with tilde, caret, wildcards, trailing-dash bounds and conjunctions, compares pre-release numbers as numbers, and answers "cannot tell" for an unknown qualifier, a version glued to a game version, or anything else it cannot read, which is never reported. The scan cache moves to format 5. `LauncherService` scans only `mods/`, with cancellation checks, and writes a warning line per issue to the launch console before the spawn, and the Content tab tints the row. Nothing is blocked. Ids every loader satisfies itself (the game, Java, the Forge, NeoForge, Fabric and Quilt loaders and their language providers, and MixinExtras, which Fabric Loader and NeoForge ship built in) are left to the loader and never reported. Build metadata after `+` is ignored, and `final`, `ga` and `release` read as the release itself.
- A Modrinth project renders natively. `Screen.ModDetail` takes a `ModTarget`, either a catalogue id or an installed file, so a jar the catalogue never indexed gets the same page with what it cannot answer marked. The page carries the description, the builds, the changelog and the gallery. `Screen.ModVersion` is one build's page, with its dependencies under requires, works with and does not work with, and its notes. `VersionsPane` is a table with channel, game version and platform filters (`VersionFilters`), the publish date shown as an age (`relativeAge`), and a build the pack cannot run marked rather than hidden. Installing works from the page for a project opened from a pack's own browser, and what the pack holds is read from the folder. `ModrinthProject` decodes the environment pair, loaders, game versions, counts, links and dates. `ModrinthClient.disclosures` reads `GET /v3/project/{id}/disclosures` into `ModrinthDisclosure`, the client's only v3 call because v2 has no equivalent. The type stays a string and the telemetry consent mode stays separate, since the vocabulary is the server's and grows. `foldGameVersions` folds supported releases by major (`1.21.x`). `LocalLinkFollower` opens a link to a project the launcher can draw on its own page instead of in a browser, and a YouTube embed in a description becomes a thumbnail card.
- A surface holds several named slot sets, its families, and shows one. `SurfaceLayout.families` maps a `FamilyId` to a `FamilyLayout` with its own slots and plane. `SlotPath` carries the family, because an address that needs runtime state to resolve is only half an address. Which family is live is runtime state in `SurfaceFamilies` and never reaches the file, so switching cannot overwrite the arrangement a reader built for the other family. Claims are counted: the shell animates two screens that want the same family across each other, and an unconditional reset dropped the family a frame after it went up. `appshell.rightrail` gains the `projectView` family, whose compatibility, links, tags, creators and details blocks are ordinary widgets in `ProjectRailWidgets.kt` reading what the project page publishes through a data source. Layout schema 10 to 11 wraps every surface's slots in the general family (`wrapSlotsInGeneralFamily`), the reconcile seeds families the bundled default gains, and the editor can arrange a family the app is not showing through `LocalFamilyOverrides`.
- The Content tab updates what an instance holds, puts any published build under a row and installs a mod with what it requires. `InstanceContentUpdater.check` asks `POST /v2/version_files/update_many` for a whole folder in chunks through `ModrinthClient.latestForHashes`. It asks one `ModUpdateChannel` rung at a time, so a mod with releases is never offered a newer alpha, and `effectiveChannel` follows the channel the installed file is already on. `updateFrom` ranks answers by publish date and rejects an answer whose primary file is the installed file, because the endpoint returns the newest matching version and a current file gets itself back, and one published before the installed file. `loadersFor` asks `minecraft` for resource packs and `iris` and `optifine` for shaders. `start` runs a batch on the app scope, fails per file and reports through the activity pill. `InstanceContentManager.replace` moves the new file in before removing the old one and keeps the row's on/off state, so a failed swap leaves both files rather than neither. A build picked by hand is the same swap through `swapFor`. `ModInstaller` follows required dependencies breadth first, honours an exact version pin and leaves a dependency already present as it is. The mod browser reads what the instance holds from the folder through `ModInstaller.installedProjects`. `ContentKind.folderName` and `DISABLED_SUFFIX` replace three copies of the folder mapping, and `ConfirmDialog` is the shared base of `DestructiveConfirmDialog`. `ContentIconStore` keeps row icons across visits, keyed by file and size, instead of hashing every jar each time the tab opens, and the fallback plate is drawn instead of the image rather than under it.
- `NxPill` takes a leading slot in place of its dot, and a border. `NxMetaChip` sets the hairline per tone and caps its label instead of shrinking it. `LoaderGlyph` draws the loader marks as stroked paths with their transform carried beside them, since Fabric's path is authored around (800, 760) and lands a thousand units off screen when read without its matrix. `Motion.track` paces a mark travelling to what it now marks. `NxStateBlock` is the shell the empty, failed and unbuilt states share, and `NxNotBuiltYet` is the unbuilt one. `NxTooltip` can centre on its anchor. Two disclosure glyphs join the bundled icon font.
- A video wallpaper can carry its own sound and can be what the player widgets drive. `BackgroundSettings.audio`, off by default, decodes the file's audio stream to a named `AudioOutput` stream. `audioVolume` sets the level on the running player, so turning it down does not reopen the file. `BgAudioWidget` carries both and joins the bundled layout after the loop mode. `linkToPlayers` makes the clip the media object. `MusicPlayerService` leaves the widget service registry and is app-provided as `PlaybackRouter`, which flattens the flows of its owner (the `AudioPlayer` adapter, or `WallpaperSession` while linked) and reads the owner at each verb. The player widgets and `MediaSessionBridge` read the contract rather than the engine, so the desktop follows whoever owns playback. `WallpaperSession` reuses the music player's state mapping. Its queue is the one clip, the verbs that move between entries are inert, and its pause stops the picture too. Sound needs a moving wallpaper and the link needs sound, and both are withheld with the reason shown. A linked wallpaper plays under `WhenUnwatched.KeepTime` rather than `Freeze`, so minimising does not stop the music. The loop mode is assigned to the running player instead of keying a new one. A reopen for sound carries the position across with a seek that waits for the decoder to leave `Opening` and lands on a keyframe: an exact seek during the hardware frame context's start-up turned a resume into a fatal `av_hwframe_transfer_data` failure.
- `SettingsData.experimentalReuseSession`, off by default behind a toggle in the SmartyCraft settings, launches an SC-bound pack with the session in hand instead of signing in again before the spawn, and makes auto-login trust the saved SmartyCraft token without a request. Every SmartyCraft login mints a new uid and retires the previous one. The per-launch login is why a two-factor account asked for a code on every launch, and the cached-password check at startup killed the token it was about to open on. A spent token surfaces as a join refusal instead. A session counts as reusable when its token is non-blank and it carries no refresh token (`reusableForSc`), not when its status is OK, because a session restored from the store has no status. Microsoft keeps its silent refresh.
- A bundled widget shipped after a layout was first written now reaches that layout once. `LayoutSeeding` places a bundled widget found in neither the graph nor `layout-seeded.json` at its bundled position, and records the offer. `SeededWidgets` is the record of every bundled id the graph has been offered, so a widget the user removes stays removed. A file from before the record counts everything the graph holds as offered, plus any bundled widget whose kind already sits in its slot. The record is a sibling file because a field in the envelope would have needed a schema bump, and a schema bump holds an older build's whole layout read-only. A read-only graph is left alone.
- The layout editor can undo and redo. `EditHistory` keeps snapshots of the immutable `LayoutGraph` rather than inverse operations: every transform rebuilds only the path it touched, so a snapshot shares nearly all of its structure with the one before, while an inverse per mutation would be wrong exactly for the pair that does not round trip. A run of edits carrying the same key within `COALESCE_MS` (400ms, longer than the repository's 200ms write debounce) is one entry holding the graph the run started at, so a drag that writes an offset per frame is one step. The key is per widget and per axis for geometry writes and null for a structural edit, which never coalesces, and the history keeps 64 entries. Resetting a surface or everything is recorded like any other edit. `EditModeController` exposes `undo`, `redo`, `canUndo` and `canRedo`. `ShellChord.UndoEdit` and `RedoEdit` claim Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Shift+Z only while an editor is open, the gate Escape uses, so the chord stays with text fields the rest of the time. The editor bar carries the pair, greyed when there is nothing to take back.
- The widget palette is a gallery that draws each widget. `WidgetPreview` renders the real widget through the real render path into an off-screen `ImageComposeScene` and caches the picture while the palette is open. A widget that refuses to compose, or composes and inks nothing, keeps its letter tile. The scene is built empty and given its content afterwards, so a throwing widget cannot throw from the constructor and leave a scene nobody can close: an unclosed scene keeps its recomposer registered as a global snapshot apply observer that every later state write walks. `ScenePool` keeps one scene per raster size for the life of the gallery, because building a scene costs 30 to 45ms against 5 to 14ms to draw into a warm one, empties a scene on its way back so a hidden widget's effects stop, and drops one whose composition threw. Previews are drawn on the composition's thread: a scene composed on a worker reached the window's own `SnapshotStateObserver` through the process-wide apply observers, and the next pointer event failed the multithreaded access check. `PreviewThreadRuleTest` refuses any shipping source that builds an off-screen scene and names a dispatcher or a thread. `PreviewEnvironment` substitutes the sources with `previewDataRegistry`, gives the preview its own `WidgetServiceRegistry`, a `DiscardedState` host and a throwaway `ShellChromeBounds`, and stands down the four editor hooks. Before this, a preview registered itself in the live service registry, where `useService` could pick it over a real instance, it persisted its state, and an empty nested slot reported scene coordinates into the live `DropTargetRegistry`, so a drop near the window's top-left corner landed on nothing. The command registry stays live, because an empty one throws from a dispatch inside the scene's scope. `STUB_SHELL` in `StubContexts` lets the chrome widgets compose there. The frame is read back in one call and trimmed to its ink once, where it used to be sampled every fourth pixel across the native boundary on the UI thread, which missed hairlines depending on the display scale. A tile takes its shape from the declared preferred size through `frameFor`, so the grid does not move as previews land, and tiles are grouped by the first segment of the kind. `OffscreenPreviewProbeTest`, `WidgetPreviewCensusTest`, `ScenePoolTest`, `PreviewIsolationTest` and `PaletteTileShapeTest` pin the net, the census, the pool, the isolation and the tile shape.
- A widget declares what it needs, wants and can use. `@Widget` gains `minWidth`, `minHeight`, `prefWidth`, `prefHeight`, `maxWidth` and `maxHeight`, all zero (undeclared) by default, carried as `WidgetSizing` on the descriptor and published to the widget through `LocalWidgetSizing`. `WidgetValidator` refuses bounds out of order and a preferred size on one axis only, because the palette tile, the drag ghost and `AdaptiveWidget` read a preferred size as a pair. Both slot branches hold a placement claim inside the declaration through `boundWidth` and `boundHeight`: a claim under the floor is raised to it, one past the ceiling stops there, and no claim means no bound, so a ceiling describing a width is never applied to the height. The resize handle stops at the same numbers and draws them as dashed guides while held, per axis only where declared and not in a lattice. A palette drop is drawn at the declared footprint without writing it into the layout as a claim. `AdaptiveWidget` takes its reference size from the declaration instead of a literal. The clock, notes, checklist and the four object players declare sizes, and the column player's height floor keeps a 272dp claim from cutting off its transport.
- A free-placed widget resizes from eight handles, four corners and four sides, each with its own cursor, and while a widget is dragged or resized whatever it overlaps is outlined in the error colour. `PlacementGeometry` resolves a leading-edge drag through the edges in slot coordinates and back to an offset by the inverse of the renderer's anchor rules, since adding the pointer delta to the stored offset is wrong on six of the nine anchors: an end anchor's offset is an inset, and a centre anchor takes half. Offset and size land in one write, so a resize is one undo step. The resize hangs the origin off the size the widget draws at rather than its stored ceiling, so growing a content-limited widget past its content no longer slides it. The gestures read the unclipped layout size and the drawn offset, so a widget under the right panel is not measured by its visible sliver. The placement clamp runs in offset space and applies the anchor's inward sign afterwards, in the renderer and the drag alike. `DropTargetRegistry.overlapping` ignores a shared edge, so a flush row is not a row of warnings. A palette drop lands inside the slot instead of hanging past its far edge. The lattice keeps its single corner handle.
- A shell region's own settings live on the region's own editor tab. Each `EditorSurfaces` entry names the region widget it is the inside of (`ownerRegion`), and its tab offers that region's width, collapse and plane directly, where widening the right rail meant finding the rail widget inside the row of regions on a frame tab. The region is found by searching the frame rather than from a table, so the shell's shape is not recorded twice. A folded rail keeps its tab, dimmed with a crossed-out eye and named as folded for a screen reader, and pressing it opens the region's settings rather than a canvas a hairline wide. `EditorSurfaces.foldedAway` reads the stored collapse prop only, because the editor renders the right rail at full width whatever the window measures. The editor state stays keyed on the set of editable tabs and the fold is asked per tab, so collapsing a rail from its own panel, or resizing the window across the rail's fold width, no longer ends the edit session, and moving between screens keeps it.
- A free-placed widget reflows to fit the room its anchor leaves in the slot. The placement branch caps the drawn width and height at `roomFromAnchor`, measured from the anchored edge and less the placement padding, so a widget wider than a slot narrowed by the right panel lays its content out smaller instead of running under the panel. It never goes below its declared floor, and a widget parked past the slot is left to the grab-margin clamp. `LocalPlacementReflow` turns the reflow off while a placement is being edited, because capping the drawn box froze the leading-edge resize handle that hangs off it. A `graphicsLayer` scale of the whole slot was dropped in its favour: it drifted end-anchored widgets off their corner and moved the canvas under the pointer during a drag. `SlotRendererGeometryTest` covers the cap.
- Release AppImages carry update information (`gh-releases-zsync|Kitty-Hivens|Nexira|latest|Nexira-x86_64.AppImage.zsync` for x86_64) and publish the `.zsync` appimagetool writes beside them, so AppImageUpdate and the integrators built on it can update the file themselves. Beta and nightly builds carry none: those tools decide an update exists by comparing hashes rather than versions, and `latest` resolves to the newest release, so either would be taken back to the release. `scripts/build-appimage.sh` fails when update information was embedded and the `.zsync` is missing, and reports it through `GITHUB_OUTPUT` as `zsync`. `build_release.yml` uploads it and records it in the manifest as kind `zsync`, and `verify-release.sh` expects it on a release. The launcher's own updater still serves every channel.
- A theme made from the wallpaper. `coloursFromImage` reads the five most characteristic colours of a still, or of a video's first frame, instead of one seed. `themeFromWallpaper` authors a `Theme` from them: Celestia's ladder and inks tone for tone, tinted toward the first colour, and the wallpaper's colours moved in tone into the band each mode needs, so both modes exist and the day and night switch keeps working. A picture without colour gives a neutral ladder and Celestia's colours. The picker offers it while there is a wallpaper. `ThemeLibrary.wallpaper` stores its colours in `themes.json` beside the selection, so a launcher that opens on it opens in it rather than in the default until the picture decodes. Removing the wallpaper drops it and the selection falls back to the default.
- Home is set in type, with the library as a list. Most packs have no art, and the banner and tiles put the pixel fill at sizes where it reads as noise. `home.new.continue` gives the pack to go back to as a name at display size that grows with its width, beside its icon or a strip of its fill, with playtime, last played and loader as numerals and Play. `home.new.packlist` lists the library like tracks (mark, name, loader, last played, a waiting build, hours, Play under the pointer) and drops its least needed columns first when narrow. It lists every pack unless its props name a ceiling. Where the slot bounds its height it scrolls inside it, with the bar in the panel's side padding clear of each row's Play, and on a page that scrolls it lays out in full. `home.new.whatsnew` lists a new launcher build and the packs with builds waiting, last played first, with each line opening the update dialog or the pack's versions screen, and draws nothing when nothing is new. The update the check finds is kept in `LauncherUpdateState`, which `UpdateManager` writes and which carries requests for its dialog back, so there is still one dialog and one install path. The bundled Home is continue, What's new and the list. `LayoutSeeding.SUPERSEDES` counts a widget as offered when its slot already holds a kind it supersedes (`home.new.hero` or `home.new.quicklaunch` for continue, `home.new.recent` for the list), so a layout that still holds those is left as it was. The hero follows its width, with its height as a floor and a ceiling at 420dp, and steps its mark and name up when tall. The recent row is a grid that fills the width at a minimum tile size, with rows and minimum width as props, and its tiles lift on a spring under the pointer and show the pack's launch control, which stays while a launch runs. A pending build shows on a tile, on the hero and on the library card through one shared badge. `HomeConceptsProbe` draws the five Home concepts (column, type, spines, dock, bento) the layout was chosen from.
- Ready-made Home arrangements. The preset dialog offers type (the bundled Home, so it is the way back), column, spines, dock and bento above the saved presets, read through `BundledPresets` from `widget/presets/home-*.json` in the jar at the schema this build reads. A shipped preset carries only the surfaces it is about and replaces those alone, so the rails, every other screen and the look stay as they were, where a saved preset is the whole layout and the whole look. `BundledPresetsTest` and `BundledPresetWidgetsTest` hold that each preset loads, names only surfaces the layout has, leaves the rest untouched, mints no duplicate ids, and places widgets this build has with props they read strictly. Two widgets came with them: `home.new.spines` sets every pack as a full-height strip on a shelf with one opened out, which is where a pack's pixel fill reads as texture, and `home.new.time` sets the time and date as the largest thing on its own plane. The hero gains `fillHeight` to stand as a column.
- A widget arrives when its surface opens. `@Widget(enter = ...)` declares one of a closed set, `Entrance` (none, fade, rise, settle), whose timing belongs to the motion scale (`Motion.fade`, `Motion.reveal`, `Motion.emphasis` with its overshoot), and `WidgetValidator` rejects an unknown name at build time. An instance can name its own and pin its own delay, stored as `motion` on the instance only when set and edited in an Entrance section of the widget panel. The kernel knows the character and the widget's place in its slot and hands both to `LocalWidgetEntrance`, a no-op by default. client-ui provides `PlayedWidgetEntrance`, which staggers a slot's widgets by place. The call sits inside each widget's movable content so toggling the editor does not replay it, and widgets start arrived in the editor. The arrival is alpha, offset and scale in the draw phase over a widget laid out at its final size, invisible from the first frame. Undeclared widgets fade, the shell regions declare none so the frame stays still, and the Home widgets declare rise.
- `NxParticleField` draws motes in the theme's colours: one of `ParticleField` (dust drifting, embers rising and burning out, snow falling and swaying, stars twinkling) in three `ParticleDensity` steps, with a ceiling on the count. Each mote is a function of its number and the time, with every property from a hash of the number, so there is no simulation and no state: a frame is one pass that allocates nothing, the same field is drawn at any moment, and it stays inside its area however long it runs. It draws in its own layer from its own frame clock and invalidates only its own draw. The `decor.particles` widget puts one anywhere a widget can go.
