The centre region renders a new `appshell.backdrop` surface under the screen, where decor shows in the gaps the screen leaves and over the wallpaper, and the editor lists it as its own tab. The bundle ships it empty, since a field spends frames and that is the reader's choice.
- Reduce motion, beside blur in Appearance, stored as `CustomizationSettings.reduceMotion` so nx-ui and the widget layer read one answer. Widgets are in place when a surface opens instead of arriving, and a particle field holds still on its first frame. Motion that says something is left alone.
- A slot says how it shows what does not fit. `ViewportSpec` beside the arrangement names `Static`, `Scroll` along one axis (optionally `paged`) or `Map`. Static is what every slot was, and what a slot naming nothing or a kind this build does not know reads as. The kernel owns the rules each surface used to decide or refuse. Along a scrolling axis a weight is a share of one screen, and a widget that declares no maximum, the stand-in for an unknown kind included, gets one screen as its ceiling. A placement slot is as long as its furthest start-attached widget, and a lattice that scrolls sideways counts rows and grows to the right. A paged slot turns one screen per wheel turn, taken before the scroll sees the event so a burst of notches is one page, and settles on the nearer page after a bar or touchpad scroll. A map is a plane with no edges holding placed widgets at their own points, negative ones included, without anchors or lattice. On a map the wheel moves the view down, Shift moves it sideways, a touchpad moves it both ways, and a drag on anything no widget claimed moves it after the touch slop. Its position is saved like a scroll position, and a corner button brings the view back once no widget is drawn inside it. A widget can be `pinned`: in a scrolling flow it stands at the start outside the part that moves, and in a scrolling placement slot or on a map it is placed against the view by its own anchor. The slot menu sets the scroll (none, down, sideways, the bar, paged), turning a lattice across the diagonal when it starts or stops scrolling sideways. A drag or resize near a page's edge scrolls it through `EdgeAutoScroll`, faster the deeper the pointer goes, with the held widget kept to the pointer and a palette drop converted against the whole slot. Layout schema 14 (`scrollTheBackgroundControls`) carries the background settings' scroll, which was hardcoded in `BgSettingsSurface`, onto saved layouts. The scrollbar is a seam the app fills with its auto-hiding bar. `SlotViewportGeometryTest` covers a widget sent far down a page already measured short, which used to creep out one widget height per frame.
- A screen of one's own. A `ScreenSpec` in the layout graph (an id, a name, an icon and the surface it opens) sits beside a surface with one slot. The editor's pill makes one: a blank static page with a `nav.screen` link at the bottom of the rail, opened with its settings in `ScreenPropertiesPanel` to rename it, pick an icon from `ScreenIcons`, or delete it with everything on it and every link to it. It is arranged, scrolled, saved and reset by the machinery the built-in screens use. A full reset keeps made screens and their content and puts their links back (`ScreenLinks`), resetting one leaves it blank, and the breadcrumb reads the name live. The context Home's widgets read is provided around the whole shell, so they work on any screen. A surface opened inside itself through a widget is refused through `LocalMountedSurfaces` and keeps its slot's footprint, where it used to recurse until the stack ran out, and the editor says so through `LocalRefusedMount`. The edit session belongs to the app, so moving between screens keeps the editor on, with the selection following to the new page. A deleted screen leaves a notice whose Restore puts it back, page and links in place, from the snapshot `ScreenTrash` took at the deletion, even after further edits. Layout schema 15 adds the screen list, so an older build opens the file read-only instead of dropping it.
- Widget modules load, switch off and come back without a restart. `WidgetModules` holds them instead of a registry built once at boot, and the composition reads the registry as state. The editor's `WidgetModulesPanel` switches a module off and on, and reads the folder again for a jar added, replaced or removed. A module that leaves draws its widgets as the unknown kind with everything they carried kept, and its class loader is closed after a grace period once the composition has drawn without it. `WidgetModuleLoader` opens each jar from a copy named by its SHA-1, so the file can be replaced or deleted while the launcher runs and a replaced jar is never read through the JDK's cached handle, and lists a switched-off module from its manifest without opening it. It names each class loader after its module, so the shell's recovery traces a crash through the stack to its module, switches it off before restarting and leaves a notice naming it, with the crash still counting toward safe mode. Every module ever loaded is remembered with its widget kinds until somebody forgets it, so the schema-bump prune no longer reads an absent module's widgets as renamed. client-ui's `desktopTest` loads the fixture jar `:widget-loader:fixtureModuleJar` builds, kept off the test classpath so it cannot resolve through the parent loader.
- A pack's own mod answers its right-click and overflow with a card instead of a menu. A mod the pack placed has no switch, cannot be removed and offers no versions, and since the banner that explained this left with the detach button, such a row read as a fault. `PackLockCard` shows the mod with its icon and version, a lock and the explanation: the pack installs and updates it, the next update undoes a change made here, and detaching hands it over at the cost of updates. An optional mod gets a variant saying it can be switched but not removed. The menu's actions become buttons: Pack settings opens the Data section, where detaching is, and Details opens the mod page. The lock lands with an overshoot, rocks, fills and sends one ring, and is drawn closed and still under reduced motion. `NxAnchoredCard` is the surface it uses, hanging from a trigger or opening at the pointer through the menu's popup shell and placement.
- `PackContentConceptsProbe` draws three answers for the pack page's content area over a wallpaper (as shipped, one plane with a ruled list, the same plane with tiles) at 1920x1080 and 2560x1440 with both rails. It takes the picture from the `nexira.probe.wallpaper` property and draws a generated field of colour when none is given.

### Changed
- The SmartyCraft news archive takes its floor straight from `IServerProtocol.loader()` behind a `dashboard-news` cache, rather than through the retired server-list service. The `SmartyNews` -> `NewsItem` mapping moves to `hivens.launcher.news.toNewsItem`, beside its only reader. Single-flight, the ten-minute SWR window and the refusal to cache an empty answer are unchanged; the store gate now asks whether the news is empty instead of whether the server list was.
- `DefaultSkinProvider` reads the client jar from the shared `libraries/net/minecraft/minecraft/<version>/` root the runtime provisioner fills, preferring the newest version directory, and falls back to the retired `clients/<pack>/bin/` tree. The old root was the only place it looked and nothing fills it any more, so a fresh install would never have found the nine default skins again; the fallback stays because somebody upgrading has that tree and may not have installed a pack yet.
- The `home.new.progress` widget binds to `ActivityRegistry` instead of the auto-sync snapshot, so it names an install, a pack update or a batch of content updates -- everything the launcher actually does in the background rather than the one branch that is gone. `Sources.AutoSync` becomes `Sources.Activity`.
- `AutoLoginCoordinator` and the login panel both target `Protocol.DEFAULT_SERVER_ID` directly. The `lastServerId` they consulted lived in `profiles.json`, which the removal deletes, and `CredentialsManager` never restored `SessionData.serverId` anyway, so the parameter had already been permanently null.
- The player kinds move out of `hivens.ui.widgets.sample` into `hivens.ui.widgets.players`, with the mini control, the shared measure and the video widget. A package named for demonstrations of the widget kernel is the wrong address for ten kinds carrying props, translations, render probes and a layout migration. Nothing persisted names a package: a layout stores a widget id and its props are a flat object keyed by field, so saved screens are untouched.
- `AudioPlayer` takes the open file through `PlaybackEngine`, an interface of ten members each of which was already a call it made, and the lane its engine touches run on as a `CoroutineDispatcher`. Everything the launcher decides about playback sat behind a constructor call to the decoder and could be reached by nothing: `AudioPlayerOrchestrationTest` now drives the queue's behaviour at its end, the repeat modes, a scrub into a released track, a refused open and the restored-queue path, with no FFmpeg on the test path. `AudioOutput` is the same seam for the stream, one abstract member wide (`sink(latency)`, with a no-argument overload at the video default).
- `PlaybackMiniControl` catches up with the stack it drives. It reads the app-provided `MusicPlayerService` like the other kinds rather than drawing a plane that asks the reader to add a music player, which was honest while the widget it deferred to existed and became nonsense when that widget was retired. Its measure is the shared `PlaybackScrubber` instead of a progress drawing with no gesture, keeping the `Info` status colour rather than the lead because the volume track sits ten points above it and two bars of one colour are one control a reader has to work out. Skips appear where there is a queue, and an empty strip opens a file from its own plane.
- An empty card opens a file through a press and a published semantics action rather than `clickable`, in one helper the ten share. `clickable` merges the semantics of everything beneath it, so an empty card collapsed into a single node and its overflow button and artwork square stopped being addressable. The press is taken only when no child took it first: the timeline card used the weaker test, so a click on the overflow of an empty card opened its panel and a file dialog together.
- The build, the bytecode and the bundled runtime move to Java 27. Every Kotlin and Java target, the `jvmToolchain` of the five multiplatform modules, the Gradle daemon criteria and the `JAVA_VERSION` of the CI workflows go from 26 to 27, and the jlink runtime follows the daemon JDK, so the packaged launcher ships on 27. Kotlin moves to 2.5.0-Beta1, the first line whose `JvmTarget` has `JVM_27`, with a note in the catalog to take 2.5.0 once it is stable, and Gradle to 9.8.0, the first release that runs on 27. The two java agents keep `release 8` for the game JVM. The CLI's native image cannot be built until a Liberica NIK or GraalVM on JDK 27 exists.
- Tests that could not fail now can. `KeyringStorageFactory.forOs` takes the OS name, so the NoOp fallback and the per-OS probes are asserted on every host instead of on an OS nothing builds for, and the nine blank-id checks across the native storages are one `requireKeyringIds`, asserted directly and through each storage on its own OS. `ProcessLaunchHandle` is internal so `TerminateEscalationTest` drives it rather than a drifted copy. The pending second-factor test tells its two responses apart and a new one observes the invalidation. The Windows asset choice is asserted with the OS reported for the test. `SmrtSyncService` takes how it opens a file for hashing, so the unreadable-jar verdict no longer switches itself off under root. `LayoutGraphRepository.writesLanded` counts writes, which the debounce tests assert instead of file timestamps. The action ring checks when an entry was stamped, the content search compares its whole result, and `NxRowHoverRenderTest` checks that the hover overlay is still drawn.
- The launcher's JVM launch profile is written once. `client-ui/build.gradle.kts` holds a shared list and two named deltas, the capped heap for the dev run and the jpackage launchers and the AppImage's uncapped heap with periodic concurrent collection and native heap trim. `PackagingExtension.appImageJvmArgs` feeds `EmitAppImageProfileTask`, which emits `NEXIRA_APPIMAGE_JVM_OPTIONS` for the AppRun, so `scripts/build-appimage.sh` no longer types its own copy, which had lost four flags. `-XX:TrimNativeHeapInterval=10000` is new in the AppImage profile: the AppRun's glibc trim threshold only trims when something else calls into the allocator, so what a burst allocated through malloc stayed resident. With the JVM trimming on a timer, an idle home screen went from 370 MB to 344 MB RSS with time to first frame unchanged. A heap cap, SerialGC and running without the class-data archive were measured in the same pass and none helped.
- `customDmg` is skipped off macOS through an `onlyIf` decided before inputs are read, instead of building a whole jpackage image and failing input validation on a bundle that is never produced there. `customRuntime` takes the source JDK's `release` file as an input by content, so an in-place JDK upgrade rebuilds the runtime image instead of shipping the old one.
- Kotlin warnings are errors. `allWarningsAsErrors` is set in the root `build.gradle.kts` block that pins `jvmTarget`, so it covers the main and test sources of every module, the multiplatform desktop targets included, and a new warning fails the build where it was introduced. The 87 the build had grown were cleared first, nearly all in tests: the `ExperimentalCoroutinesApi` opt-in on nine test classes, one `Json` held per test instead of built per use, a test scope in place of `GlobalScope`. `client-easter` drops `-XXLanguage:+NestedTypeAliases`, an internal flag that is itself a warning, by moving `AprilFoolsEngine`'s `Event` alias to file level. `buildSrc` holds its Java target to the JVM 26 Kotlin compiles it to. javac and KSP are outside the flag.
- `NewsChannelRenderTest` and `CompactNewsFeedChannelTest` build and draw their scenes on the event thread through `onEventThread` in `SceneSettle.kt`. The rail subscribes a `snapshotFlow`, and Compose resumes those collectors from its global apply pump on the event thread, so a scene drawn from the test thread had two threads in it, which a nightly Windows run met as `LayoutNode not found in RectList` thrown from `ImageComposeScene.render`.
- Dependencies: ktor 3.5.2 to 3.6.0, slf4j 2.0.18 to 2.0.20, logback 1.6.3 to 1.6.5, org.jetbrains markdown 0.7.9 to 0.7.16, KSP 2.3.11 to 2.3.12, material3 1.12.0-alpha03 to 1.13.0-alpha01 with the Compose line moved to 1.13.0-alpha01 to state what already resolved (material3 pulls its own core line, so the classpath carried 1.13.0-alpha01 while the catalog said 1.12.0), filekit 0.15.0 to 0.16.0, jsvg 2.1.0 to 2.2.0, skinema 0.8.0 to 0.8.2, the buildconfig plugin 6.0.10 to 6.1.2, the versions plugin 0.61.0 to 0.64.0 and `JetBrains/qodana-action` 2026.2.0 to 2026.2.1. The docs site moves to Starlight 0.42.4 and Astro 7.3.5. skinema 0.8.2 replaces `PcmSink.open(sampleRate)` with `open(format)`: the `SystemAudioOutput` adapter maps the declared encoding instead of assuming interleaved S16LE stereo, and `EnvelopeSink` reads the stride the stream declares instead of a constant four bytes a frame, which was right only for S16 stereo and would have drifted the clock rather than failed.
- The root build forwards every `nexira.probe.*` system property to the forked test JVM through `providers.systemPropertiesPrefixedBy`, which also registers the read with the configuration cache. Gradle does not pass `-D` to a test worker, so a render probe asked for by property, the invocation the probes document, quietly did not run. In `gradle.properties` both daemons now run G1 with `G1PeriodicGCInterval=60000` and a 512 MB initial heap, where the Kotlin daemon ran ParallelGC and the Gradle daemon started at 2 GB: ParallelGC effectively never returns a page, so two idle daemons held about three gigabytes between builds. Test JVMs keep ParallelGC, and a `gradle.properties` under `GRADLE_USER_HOME` still overrides the whole block.
- A slot arranges its children by a `FlowSpec` or hands each one a `Placement`, and the five `SlotOrientation` names are gone. They were two ideas: Column, Row and Grid are one flow (`direction`, `wrap`, `uniform`), and Canvas and the cube grid are one placement with and without quantisation, which `SlotContent.grid` now states as the unit (0 is one dp, and N is one cell of an N-column lattice, so a cell address survives a window resize). `Placement` carries the anchor, offset, size, `z` and `weight`, replacing three per-widget fields of which at most one ever meant anything. The anchor fixes a shipped defect: every free-placed widget was positioned from the top left in absolute dp, so an arrangement made on a wide window was clipped on a narrow one. The anchor and the direction are strings read with a fallback, so the layout model no longer uses `LenientEnumSerializer` and its wire format carries no enum. Layout schema 9 to 10 runs in the new `JsonMigrations` before the decoder, because the shared Json ignores unknown keys and a step on the decoded side would have returned every Row, Grid and Canvas as a plain column. A hand-edited zero column count is clamped where the old renderer clamped it, and malformed slot input throws in both shapes so the repository falls back to the bundled default. The renderer clamps what it draws through `clampPlacementAxis`, solved from the anchor, for the lattice and free placement alike, and the drag uses the same function, so a column count stepped down or an offset from a wider window stays within reach while the record keeps its value. `SlotRenderer` reads the lattice cell through `BoxWithConstraints` during composition instead of through `onSizeChanged` a frame later, which drew every widget at the origin on a slot's first frame. Picking an anchor resets the offset, the anchor is chosen from a 3x3 grid in the widget menu, and the lattice resize handle quantises to whole cells. Presets go through the same ladder and are refused below the surface schema or from a newer build instead of being stripped and written back. The bundled default refuses to load when its stamp disagrees with `LAYOUT_SCHEMA`. `CanvasGeometry` becomes `PlacementGeometry`.
- `VersionPickerWindow` sizes its list and detail pane in fixed numbers rather than proportions of the app: a card capped at 928 by 550, a 300-wide rail, and a fixed-height row carrying channel, number and badge, with the date, runtime, size and notes in the pane. At 88 percent of the window on both axes, eight builds sat over four hundred empty pixels. Builds that cannot run here are counted and folded behind a toggle over the list's fade instead of filtered out, since filtering made the question of whether a build exists for this loader impossible to ask. "Latest" marks the newest build that runs here (`pickerVersionsOf`). Nothing is preselected, a notes line that only repeats the version is skipped, and the rail no longer matches the search field inside it. `NxSteadyText` measures text at its bold width and draws it at the current weight, so a chip whose weight toggles no longer shifts the row beside it.
- Release notes render through `ReleaseNotes`, which maps the whole Markdown heading ladder onto the app's type scale and its colours onto the theme. The renderer set `#` in displayLarge, so a changelog opening with its own version number printed it at 36sp above a pane titled at 16sp. The version picker, `PackVersionsScreen` and `UpdateDialog` read through it.
- `AbstractCachingAuthProvider.CacheKey` drops the server id, so the SmartyCraft session cache is keyed by account. The SC game token is not scoped to the world it was minted for, and keying by server made every world switch a fresh login, which on a two-factor account demands another code and invalidates the session just confirmed. A hit for another server re-stamps `serverId` on the returned session, so the authlib choice still matches the launch.
- The customization studio's prop panel draws its rows on `NxRow`, which gains `compact` and `labelWidth`, instead of on its own row, field, colour input and heading. The panel had three left edges (a switch at the far edge, fields at 140, sliders at 150) and now has one. A colour prop can be cleared again: the panel's own hex field emitted only non-blank values while the prop documents the empty string as falling back to the theme, and `NxColorField` reports blank. The docked panels draw on `NxSurface` instead of a hand-drawn shadow and clip, which had no hairline.
- `LaunchTarget` is a class instead of a sealed interface whose only case was `Pack`, so the vacuous `is LaunchTarget.Pack` branches in `LaunchDriver` and the offline-relaunch guard go. The `onSessionUpdated` callback the classic home owned leaves `HomeNewContext`, the stub contexts and the shell, which read the session off the authenticated state. The project page copies its link through `LocalClipboard` instead of the deprecated `LocalClipboardManager`.
- The editor no longer recomposes the shell on every frame of a gesture. `LocalLayoutGraph` was a `staticCompositionLocalOf`, which recomposes the whole subtree it is provided over on any change, and a drag writes a new graph per pointer move, so every widget on every surface, both rails, the top bar and an open gallery recomposed sixty times a second to move one box. It is a dynamic local now. The reporter handed to the static `LocalSlotBoundsReporter` is remembered instead of rebuilt inline, and the palette reads the live drag state for its dimming, so the editor host's body no longer does.
- Widget padding moves from the plane to the placement. `WidgetSurface` applied `SurfaceSpec.padding` as an inset that shrank the plane inside its footprint, and a widget that paints its own plane, the players among them, never reached it. `Placement.padding` is applied by the slot renderer as outer spacing in both slot modes and reserves room around the widget. The prop panel shows a Padding section for every widget above Backing, which loses its padding rows. The Home slot drops its blanket 24 by 20 inset, so a placed widget can reach the window edge, and the bundled default seeds the same gutter onto its widgets. Layout schema 12 to 13 (`compensateHomeSlotPadding`) puts it back on each placed widget of a saved Home arrangement, on the side its anchor counts from, so nothing moves. `SurfaceSpec.padding` is still decoded but no longer drawn, and no migration step moves a stored plane padding onto the placement.
- The capped launch profile (dev run, Windows and macOS) lowers `-Xms` from 128m to 64m and sets `MinHeapFreeRatio=10` and `MaxHeapFreeRatio=30`, so committed heap follows the live set down after a real GC: a boot measured a 44 MB live set with 133 MB committed, because G1 does not uncommit below `-Xms`. The profile carries no periodic idle GC. A concurrent periodic cycle returned nothing on an idle launcher, since G1 uncommits only at the end of a stop-the-world pause, and the stop-the-world form froze the audio feed thread every interval, which drained skinema's ring buffer and underran the music. The music sink now asks libsound for `LatencyProfile.RELAXED` (200ms) through `AudioOutput.sink(latency)`, which rides out an ordinary GC pause, and the wallpaper keeps the shorter default, where the sound tracks the picture.
- `WaveformStrip`, `WaveformColumn` and `WaveformRing` reduce the envelope through a `BarsMemo` remembered per envelope, which recomputes only when the bar count changes. The reduction ran inside the draw lambda and allocated a new `FloatArray` on every draw, which a video wallpaper holding the frame clock at display refresh turned into steady allocation.
- Render probes settle instead of drawing a fixed number of frames. `ImageComposeScene.settle` in `SceneSettle.kt` advances the clock and sleeps between frames as before, stops rendering once the scene has gone four frames without asking for one, and resumes if something wakes it, which takes the six slowest probes from 82 to 32 seconds. Thirty-three test sites now close the frames they discard, each a skia raster held in native memory until it was collected. `MarkdownRenderTest` renders until text is on the page and the scene stops asking for frames, with a fifteen second deadline, since the markdown and HTML parses run off the composition and on a slow Windows runner thirty frames were not always enough. `LineLayoutCacheTest` starts and closes an empty `ImageComposeScene` before each test, because a `TextMeasurer` needs the platform text implementation that a scene registers when it starts, so the class failed when run alone.
- The AppImage update replaces the file the launcher was started from, under the name it already has, and release assets are named by channel rather than version: `Nexira-<arch>.AppImage` for release and beta, `Nexira-nightly-<arch>.AppImage` for a nightly. `LinuxUpdateApplicator` used to install under the published file name and then rewrite desktop shortcuts to follow it, so a store or integrator that renamed or moved the file, or a menu entry naming it, lost the launcher, and a nightly kept beside a release could be overwritten by the other's update. The rename path and the shortcut rewrite are gone, and `leftoversIn` sweeps the staged file under the running name as well as any `*.AppImage.new` an older build left. `UpdateService.linuxAssetName` asks for the channel's name exactly, so a second AppImage in one release is never taken, and falls back to the single AppImage of a release published before these names. `scripts/build-appimage.sh` picks the name and reports it through `GITHUB_OUTPUT`, and the release body reads it from the checksum list. `DesktopIntegration.healEntry`, run by `UpdateManager` at startup, rewrites `dev.hivens.nexira.desktop` only when the AppImage it names no longer exists, and creates no entry.
- A theme declares what the interface may be drawn with, and nx-ui decides where each colour goes. The old record named eight colours after places in the interface, and the pipeline seeded a generated scheme from the accent and pasted four of the eight back over it, so a preset's declared ground never reached the screen and any wallpaper made all nine presets look alike. `Theme` now carries a dark `Scheme` and an optional authored light one: a ladder of `steps` from the page outward, two `inks` and its `colors` in order of emphasis, with no field named after a use. Screens ask for ink through `NxInk` (`main`, `quiet`, `off`, `line`) and for colour with a meaning through `NxColor` (`lead`, a closed `Status` set, a feature enum implementing `ColorCategory`, `hashed`, `on(fill)`, `wash`). Every answer is fitted to the plane it is drawn on by moving its tone only, to 3:1 as a mark and 4.5:1 as text, and a status colour is made in the theme's typical chroma and tone when the theme has nothing near its conventional hue. A surface names what it is through `SurfaceKind` (Page, Chrome, Panel, Card, Field, Popup, Dialog, Notice) instead of an absolute level. Depth is relative to the `Plane` that holds it, a body is opaque, and every surface hands its content the plane it sits on through `LocalPlane`, so text is made readable against that. `OnFill` does the same for content laid on a filled colour, which replaces the white hard-coded on the accent, on statuses and on brand badges. client-ui and client-easter move onto it whole. Chrome (the rail and the title bar) is solid in the step above what it frames, over a picture as well. Every widget that declared a translucent plate now declares a solid one, since text straight on a wallpaper or on a thin coat over it read only as well as the picture allowed. The mod page header and tabs, the theme picker title, the library empty state, the recent packs row, the pack page's content toolbar and its worlds tab sit on panels of their own, and a layout can still make any of them see-through. A wallpaper is darkened with the theme's page colour rather than black. The picker draws each theme as a small piece of interface rendered inside it, and the preview as a real section, field, chips, buttons and card, both through the same function the launcher draws with. A theme with no light scheme says so in light mode. Celestia keeps the hand palette the interface was measured against, both modes, and the other presets in `Themes` are what their authors declared, dark only for now. `ThemeManager` holds a `ThemeLibrary` (the shipped themes, the wallpaper theme and the user's own) in `themes.json` schema 2. A first-version file naming a preset selects it, and one naming anything else becomes the user's own theme with every colour it held. A layout written with the old fill names (`sunken`, `base`, `raised`, `floating`) is read as `field`, `panel`, `card` and `popup`, and new layouts are written with the new words. `ThemeIntentTest` replaces `PaletteEngineTest`, `PaletteSpecTest`, `ThemeGroundTest` and `TextContrastTest`.
- Pack settings open as a sheet over the whole window from its right edge. The window was mounted inside the centre pane, so its scrim stopped at the rails and the title bar, it was sized as a share of that pane, and its rows mixed two type scales. `PackSettingsWindow` becomes `PackSettingsSheet` on the new `NxSideSheet`, which keeps one width on any display, widens on request, and plays the same exit for Esc, the scrim and the close button. Sections are tabs along its top on `NxTabRow`, the project page's travelling tab row moved into nx-ui, which `ModDetailScreen` now draws through too. A section comes in from its tab's side while the one being left fades in place. Every section is rebuilt on `NxSettingGroup` with `NxSettingRow` and `NxSettingBlock`, one row shape for every setting, which eases to a new height when its detail text changes. `RamSelector` is Auto or a custom value in gigabytes on a slider bounded by what the machine can spare, with an exact field that says when a value is out of range instead of dropping it. The custom slider, the own Java path, the window size fields and the update banner open out under the row that reveals them. The header and General show the loader with its version, the Content section is hidden for a pack that cannot have optional content, and the danger zone title is in the error colour. `PackSettingsConceptsProbe` keeps the three placements (window, sheet, page tab), drawn at 1920x1080 and 2560x1440.

### Removed
- The SmartyCraft raw server-list path is gone whole. `SmartyCraftServerListService`, `ServerListCacheStore`, `FileDownloadService`, `AutoSyncService`, `ManifestCache`, `ManifestProcessorService`, `ProfileManager`, `SmartyModPlanner`, `OpenSmrtHelperResolver`, `ModInjector`, `ClasspathProvider`, `NeoForgeVersionDetector`, `ClientSyncCoordinator`, `ClientRootDirs` and `ServerNameValidator` go with the contracts `IFileDownloadService`, `IManifestProcessorService`, `IInstanceProfileStore`, `IManifestStore`, `IServerListService`, the models `ServerProfile`, `ServerSource`, `DashboardData`, `SyncProgress`, `SmartyResponse`, the `ServerRepository` adapter and the `ILauncherService.launchClient` / `launchClientWithLogs` pair. SmartyCraft arrives as mirror packs, so the second install-sync-launch mechanism was duplicating the pack path with weaker guarantees: no manifest snapshot on the instance, no compat grading, and no roster the launch holds the contents to. SmartyCraft authentication, its 2FA gate, the authlib-redirect agent and the SC authlib swap are untouched -- those belong to the account, not to the retired transport.
- The screens that fronted it: `DashboardScreen`, `ServerSettingsScreen`, `ServerProfileResolution`, `LaunchControlPanel`, `ServerGrid`, `ServerPill`, `SquareServerCard`, and the `home.classic` / `server.details` surfaces with their six widgets. Layout schema 11 -> 12: `dropRetiredServerLayout` drops both surfaces from a saved graph and reaps the five retired widget kinds wherever they sit, including inside a container's child slots. The kinds are reaped in the JSON ladder rather than left to `WidgetGraphReconciler`'s prune, because that pass runs only after a schema bump and only on the layout file -- a preset carrying a retired kind would otherwise write it into a graph that is already current, where nothing could ever reap it.
- The spawn-point reset goes with the screen that offered it: `PlayerRepository`, `IServerProtocol.spawn`, its `SmartycraftV1Protocol` implementation, the `SpawnRequest` body and `SmartycraftSignatureBuilder.forSpawn`. The upstream action stays documented in `docs/dev/smartycraft-v1-protocol.md` because it is SmartyCraft's API and not ours; the launcher simply no longer calls it. The signature tests keep the four-field scheme through `forTwoAuth`, golden vector included, since it is the scheme under test rather than any one action.
- The home-view setting and its `SettingsData.homeView` field along with the `HomeView` enum: there is one home to choose from. Same for `autoSyncAllPacks`, `useOpenSmrtHelper` and `strictModVerification`, read only by the retired sync. A settings file from an older build decodes without them through `ignoreUnknownKeys`, which `RetiredSettingsValueTest` now pins for both shapes of loss: an unknown enum constant and an unknown field. `LaunchError.OfflineNoManifest` and `LaunchError.HelperUnavailable` were reachable only from that sync and go with their copy.
- `ProtectedPaths` and its `protected-paths.json` go too. The list of names the sync must never overwrite belonged to the retired path, which downloaded whatever the server listed with no record of what it had written before, and its own KDoc said it retires with that path. The mirror path never read it, since it keeps the installed manifest as a baseline and tells a shipped file from a player's edit on that evidence. It was still registered in DI with nothing injecting it. `RetiredDataSweeper` now takes the file with the other bookkeeping once the last client goes, and `Storage.PROTECTED_PATHS_FILE` is dropped.
- The palette generator (`PaletteEngine`, `PaletteSpec` and their variants), `CustomizationSettings.accentOverride`, and the switch that seeded the palette from the wallpaper with its `SettingsData.paletteFromWallpaper` field. The theme owns its colours outright, so there is no later step for an accent override to paste into, and nothing wrote it. The wallpaper no longer colours a preset: it is a theme of its own. An older settings file decodes without the field through `ignoreUnknownKeys`.
- The whole-buffer console document builder. `buildConsoleDoc` and `styleDoc` built the console as one annotated string, which the per-line canvas replaced: `buildLineModels` runs the same filter and span pass with line-local offsets and the layout cache colours each visible line, and nothing called the old pair. They go with `ConsoleDoc`, `ConsoleRender`, `DocSpan` and `LineSeverity`, `ConsoleRenderTest`, whose structural cases `LineModelTest` covers, and the ignored `ConsolePerfBench` that measured the old path. `SpanRole`, `spanStyleFor` and `findAllSubstring` stay, since the live path uses them.
- `worldBounds`, `fitOrtho`, `renderScene` and `Bounds3` from `scene3d/Camera.kt`, with `CameraTest`. Nothing but their own test called them. Framing the figure is not the view's job: whoever places `SkinView3D` gives it its size through the modifier and its framing through the framing parameter. `OrthoCamera`, which `SkinView3D` uses, stays.

### Fixed
- `MediaSessionBridge` is driven by the player's own flows instead of a 500ms timer. The timer was a delay added to every change and not only to the position: a pause pressed in the launcher went on reading as playing in a media widget until the next tick, and so did a skip, a repeat mode and a track change. Three defects fell out of making it a stream, each a case the timer used to skip past by luck. The loudness is the one input a hand moves directly and the player passes every pointer frame through, so a drag became sixty property-change signals a second on the session bus and is now sampled. The track's identity moved in two steps, with `_queueIndex` written before an FFmpeg open and the state that names the file only after it, so anything watching rather than sampling saw the new queue entry described with the previous entry's file. And a track running into the next one was published as paused, because the engine reports its end for the gap between two files and that maps to a state every desktop protocol reads as paused.
- The position stood still in every widget on the bus. MPRIS leaves it out of property-change notifications deliberately, because a number that moves continuously would be a message per tick, so a reader polls the property and what it reads is the last state published. Withholding a publish because only the position had moved therefore froze the scrubber while the audio played on. Three more things go out with it now. The player's own volume, which sat at the type's default of full, so a widget's slider stood at the top whatever the player was set to. `canPause` as a capability rather than as the current transport. And a seek is checked against the track id its sender named, which the command carries for exactly that and which was being discarded.
- A press on a measure lands every time, and a drag keeps being answered. The column read the duration out of the playback state from inside its seek callback, and the state is a new value on every poll, so the pointer handler keyed on that callback was torn down and rebuilt five times a second. The ring rejected a press outside the object and then went on rejecting every move after it, so a hand sweeping round a small disc froze the position wherever the pointer crossed an edge. The shared scrubber kept the previous track's length, because its gesture coroutine is keyed on whether seeking is possible at all and stepping between two loaded tracks never restarts it. And the tile drew a measure that could not be touched, which is the complaint the cards were rebuilt over. The strip and the column ran a tap detector and a drag detector on one element, and the drag won often enough that a plain click did nothing about half the time. Each now has one detector that reports the press and every move after it.
- Scrubbing into a track that finished no longer starts it silently. The poll loop drops the engine of a track that ran out, so a scrub back into it rebuilds one, and the rebuild silenced it without pausing it while skinema begins playing as soon as it is constructed: the track ran on inaudibly with its position climbing and the loop reported that as playing. `mapPlaybackState` now asks `started` of the sounding states as well as of the paused one, so the invariant does not rest on which of the silence and the pause lands first.
- One file shows under one name. The stand-in for a track with no tags was built in two places that disagreed by an extension, so an untagged file was named one way while its container was being read and another way once it had been, and a third time if the playback then failed. `elapsedLabel` reads `Ready`'s real position rather than a hard zero, which the bar beside it was already doing, so a scrub before pressing play no longer moves the bar while the clock stays at 0:00.
- `CoverPlayerCard` draws its transport glyph in `NxColor.on(lead)` rather than a hardcoded white. The dark lead is a light purple and the ink on it is black: white measures 2.65:1 against it, under even the floor for large graphics, where the theme's ink gives 7.93:1. The same card drops the elapsed timecode after the total rather than before it, which is the order its own comment described and the order every sibling kind uses.
- `SystemAudioOutput` and `MediaSessionBridge` are given back when the process exits. Neither was closed: they are singletons nobody disposes and stopping the dependency container does not call close on what it built, so the connection to the sound server and the session on the bus were released by the process dying. Each registers its hook at the moment it has something to release, so a launcher nobody played anything in installs nothing. An engine that fails to open also gives back the stream it was handed, which matters because what reliably fails there is a natives bundle that will not load and that does not improve on the next press.
- `WindowMaximizer` records the bounds and the work area it decided from. The flag is the only timestamped signal the launcher produces when the window's geometry moves underneath it, and it logged the conclusion and threw the evidence away: a tear across the lower part of the window after a resume left one line saying the window had stopped being maximized, which is three different faults and no way to tell them apart.
- `PackSnapshotService.restore` removes an instance state file the snapshot did not hold. The capture takes `.nexira-mods` and `.nexira-sync-source` only when they exist, and an instance installed before the roster existed has none, so the update wrote the first roster and the rollback kept it. That roster names the build being undone, and on an instance with no registry baseline it is the next launch's delete list, which then removed the restored build's own mods. A state file absent from the snapshot was absent before the update, so it is now removed the same way as a managed path the apply created.
- A launch the manifest does not bind to a server no longer carries the session in hand. `LauncherController.preparePackLaunch` answered "is this launch bound" twice: the guards (roster sweep, `JvmArgPolicy`, the attach guard, the environment seal, the interpreter check, the natives rebuild, the pre-spawn seal and the watchdog) read the manifest's own `auth` block, while the token came from `PackAuthRouter`, which falls back to Microsoft for every unbound pack. With the Microsoft provider unregistered, which is every shipped build, that fallback returned `currentSession` as is, so a Local, Modrinth or imported pack, or a mirror pack without an `auth` block, put a live SmartyCraft token on the command line of a game whose `mods/` nothing had checked, and that token is not scoped to one server. An unbound launch now gets a Microsoft session only when that provider is registered and signed in, and otherwise goes offline with `LaunchLogEvent.UnboundOffline` saying why in the console. A declared Microsoft requirement with no provider goes offline too instead of borrowing the session in hand. Because the unbound path no longer runs through `preparePackAuth`, a registered provider with no account no longer refuses a pack that never asked for one. `PackAuthRouter` stops deriving an SC binding from `PackOrigin.Smartycraft`: nothing creates that origin since the server-list path went, and the derived binding was the other half of the same split, a live SC session plus the redirect with every guard off. The service now receives the manifest snapshot itself rather than a copy carrying the router's answer, so the SC binding, the redirect and the guards all read one declaration.
- Signing in offline from the profile works, and survives a restart. The SmartyCraft section's `LoginPanel` looked the face up again through `AccountStore.faceSession` on every sign-in and threw away the session it was handed, but an offline identity is never stored as an account (a blank token no-ops `saveAccount`), so the lookup found nothing and the press did nothing beyond saving `offlinePlayerName`. The offline session is now passed straight to the shell. `AutoLoginCoordinator` restores that identity from `offlinePlayerName` when no account is saved, rather than only while the global offline toggle is on, since the name is the only record of it. Signing out of an offline identity clears the name, or startup would sign it straight back in.
- Interrupted-update recovery finishes before the auto-update pass starts, and rolls back only a marker that is still there once it holds the instance. `ShellStartup` launched the two as separate coroutines, and `InstanceMutationLock` makes them exclusive without ordering them, while `ApplyRecovery` took its `listPending()` before waiting for the lock. When the update got the lock first it committed and cleared the marker, and the recovery that followed restored the older snapshot over the build that had just landed. The two now run in one coroutine, recovery first, and a recovery that throws skips the pass for the session rather than updating an instance it could not vouch for. `ApplyJournal.read` re-reads the marker under the lock, and a marker gone by then is left alone.
- A cancelled or failed pack update is rolled back to the end, and a rollback that fails keeps its journal marker. `PackUpdateService.applyUpdate` caught `Throwable`, so a cancellation ran the restore inside a cancelled coroutine: `IPackRepository.put` is suspending and threw straight after the files were restored, leaving the files on the old build and the registry on the new one, and `journal.complete` then cleared the marker either way, so nothing would look at the instance again. The snapshot, the marker and the rollback now live in `ApplyGuard`, which restores under `NonCancellable`, rethrows the original failure, logs a restore that fails instead of only attaching it to a cancellation nobody logs, and in that case keeps the marker so the next start's `ApplyRecovery` gets a second attempt. `SmrtPackClient.invalidatePack` moves after the commit and out of it, since a cache that failed to clear is no reason to undo an update that landed.
- `MrpackInstaller` writes a changed override to a staged sibling and moves it into place, and `PackFileRecord.write` publishes through `AtomicFiles`. `PackSnapshotService` captures by hardlink, which holds the pre-update bytes only while a changed file arrives as a new inode, and `UnpackBudget.copyTo` opened the destination with `TRUNCATE_EXISTING`, so a Modrinth rollback restored every override the new version had touched with the new version's content. The record had the same shape and now needs it, since the Modrinth path captures it. The staging uses the `moveAtomic` helper that had been sitting unused.
- `MrpackInstaller.update` provisions the runtime first, before any file in the instance is written. It ran after the overrides were applied and the retired files dropped, so a provisioning failure left the directory on the new version while the registry still named the old Minecraft and loader, and a launch in that window built the old loader's command line over the new mods. `archivePaths` names every path a version places, for the snapshot taken ahead of it.
- `ModrinthPackUpdater.applyUpdate` runs inside the same `ApplyGuard` as the mirror path. It took a snapshot for a structural change only and had no journal, no rollback and no prune, so an installer that threw left a half-converted instance and an unclaimed snapshot, a crash left one nothing would ever return to, and snapshots piled up without bound. Every update now snapshots first, with the managed set known before anything is touched: the old record's paths, or the installed archive's for an instance without one, the new archive's paths from `MrpackInstaller.archivePaths`, and `.nexira-pack` itself, so a rollback puts back the record describing the build it restores. `rollback` counts the record as managed too, so an older snapshot that did not capture it drops the current one rather than leaving a record of the build being undone. `ModrinthPackUpdaterTest` covers the path end to end for the first time.
- A pack is not launched while its files are being rewritten, and the auto-updater leaves alone a pack that is in use. `InstanceWorkRegistry` in `client-core` records per instance which `InstanceWork` is in progress (an update or rollback, a repair, crash recovery, a batch of per-file content updates), marked by `PackOperationService`, `PackAutoUpdateService`, `ApplyRecovery` and `InstanceContentUpdater` for exactly as long as the work runs. Nothing on the launch path used to ask: `canPlay` read only whether another launch was active, the auto-update pass was invisible to every screen, and `preparePackLaunch` built a command over files an apply was replacing. `LauncherController` now refuses a busy instance with `LaunchError.InstanceBusy`, which covers the launches that do not come through a button (a notification's relaunch, the second-factor retry), and names the pack in `runningPackInstanceId` from the moment the launch is accepted rather than from the spawn, since preparing already reads the instance. The auto-update pass skips the pack that is launching or running and any pack already under other work.
- The spawn no longer writes back the record captured before the click. `onSpawned` stamped `lastPlayedEpochOrZero` onto the whole `PackInstance` the launch started from, so an update or a settings edit committed during preparation (a sign-in, a catch-up repair) was reverted: pin, baseline and cached manifest went back to the build the update had left. It re-reads the record and changes the one field, and `resolveOrFetchManifest` writes its snapshot the same way.
- Every launch control names why Play is not on offer, from one decision. `rememberLaunchControl` (client-ui `components/LaunchControl.kt`) reads the pack's launch indication, `InstanceWorkRegistry`, whether another launch holds the controller, whether the instance folder exists and whether there is anyone to play as, and resolves them through `launchBlockFor` in `client-core` into a label, an icon and an action. The pack hero, `HomeNewHero`, `HomeNewQuickLaunch` and `LaunchButtonWidget` all draw from it, where each used to decide for itself: the pack page went grey without a word when nobody was signed in and ignored the offline identity the home widgets used, and the launch tile said `launchTileBlocked` for every reason at once. That key is gone. A missing sign-in is the one block the control acts on, sending the player to the profile through `NavRequests`. `PackDetailState.play` goes with the per-screen launch path it served.
- `PlayButton` takes a state instead of two flags. `PlayTone` (Ready, Waiting, Unavailable, Problem) names what the control is doing, `progress` the share of work it waits on, `PlayGround` (Media, Surface) what it is drawn over, and `PlayLayout` (Plate, Caption) where a state's words go; the plate decides everything visual from those. Four defects in the old one, all visible in `PlayButtonStatesProbe`'s sheets: the busy plate was the ink at 0.78, so a light theme's white plate let the hero art through under grey text; the disabled ghost was white on the assumption of a darkened picture, which left a refusal next to invisible on the quick-launch card's light surface; a failure and a merely unavailable control looked the same; and the transport glyphs sat on the outline axis. Waiting now keeps the plate opaque and draws the work inside it through the new `Modifier.workProgress` (a fill up to the share, or a band crossing it on `Motion.sweep` when the size is unknown), with the share in tabular figures. Over a surface the ink is the theme's inverse, since a near-black plate on a near-black card was held apart by its hairline alone. The corner is `MaterialTheme.shapes.medium` instead of a local constant. `LaunchControl` carries the tone and the progress, read from `PackOperationService` for an update or a repair, from `InstanceContentUpdater.runs` for a content batch and from the launch indication while a download runs. `HomeNewHero` and `HomeNewQuickLaunch` gain a `playLayout` prop, Plate by default, and `LaunchButtonWidget` speaks the same language at its own size through `LaunchTile`, with its ink taken from `OnFill` and `NxColor.on` over the lead instead of a hard-coded white.
- Stopping a game waits for it to exit before the launcher reopens, and records its session. `LauncherController.abort` set Idle and cancelled the launch while the game still had up to the eight-second termination grace, so Play came back with the old JVM alive (a second launch shared its instance, one world and one log), and the cancellation threw through the only writer of playtime. With a game up it now moves to the new `LaunchState.Stopping`, calls `terminate`, and leaves the launch's own tail to record the session through `LiveSession` and settle to Idle once `awaitExit` returns; a launch still preparing is cancelled as before. The content watchdog takes the same route: `Stopping` at once, `ContentChangedDuringLaunch` raised by the tail after the process has gone, instead of an Error that read as Play inside the grace. `launchInternal` claims the gate, the abort token, the launch tag and a lazily started job in one `synchronized` step that `abort` also takes, where the identity used to be written after the lock was released and a stop in that window cancelled the previous launch and let this one run to a game the person had stopped. A stop that lands while the process is being started ends it as soon as it exists. `abort(instanceId)` is what a pack's own control calls, so a control left showing Stop cannot end another pack's game, and `LaunchDriver`'s cancellation arm clears the indication and the session registry entry as its failure arm does, unless the same pack's next launch already owns them. `PostLaunchGate` treats `Stopping` as still inside the session, so a failure reported on the way out still raises the window the gate iconified.
- The modern loader installer can be cancelled, and one cache directory is installed into by one resolve at a time. `ModernInstallerResolver.runInstaller` parked on `process.waitFor(20, MINUTES)`, which no coroutine cancellation reaches, and nothing killed the installer when its launch was stopped; the next launch then found no completion marker and ran `deleteTree` over the directory the first installer was still writing. The wait is `runInterruptible` now, a cancelled one kills the installer and its descendants, and `ensureInstalled` runs under a per-directory `Mutex` shared across resolver instances. The test stands a shell script in for the JVM and checks it never gets to finish.
- Quitting with a game running asks what to do with it, and records the session either way. The tray's Exit and the window's close with no tray to hide into called `exitApp` directly, so the game kept running with its output readers gone and its playtime unrecorded, while `AppCoroutineScopeHook`'s KDoc claimed the shutdown wound the launch down. Every way out now goes through a `quit` in `AppShellContent` that raises `QuitWithGameHost` when a pack is launching or running. Leaving the game running calls `LauncherController.settleSessionForQuit`, which writes the playtime so far from the same `LiveSession` mark the exit tail counts from, so nothing is counted twice; stopping it calls `abort` and waits, bounded at fifteen seconds, for the launch to settle. The hook's documentation now says it does not end a running game, on purpose: "leave it running" has to hold, and a launcher killed from outside leaves the game running and its session unrecorded.
- The content watchdog watches `mods/` even when it does not exist at the spawn. `LaunchContentWatchdog.run` returned an empty result for a missing directory, so a bound pack whose `mods/` had been deleted passed the pre-spawn check (nothing foreign to list, and a missing file is not a substitution), got its token, and ran with no watch at all: a `mods/` created after the spawn with a jar in it reached the loader unseen. `DirectorySnapshot` already lists a missing directory as empty, so its appearance is now a change like any other, and a pack that never has a `mods/` still settles clean.
- Pack settings write what was edited onto the record as it stands, not the copy the window was showing. The settings window (now `PackSettingsSheet`) wrote `current.instance` whole after its settle delay and again on close, so playtime recorded at exit, a build an update had committed or `lastPlayed` from a spawn during an edit went back to what the window had read. Sections now hand the window a `PackEdit`, a function of the record naming only the fields they change, and the window applies it through the new `IPackRepository.update`, which `XodusPackRepository` runs under its own lock so a concurrent writer is never lost between the read and the write. The interface's default is a plain read then write for the test fakes. `LauncherController`'s four field writers (optional content, last played, playtime, the cached-manifest backfill) moved onto it too, where they had been re-reading and then writing in two steps.
- A Modrinth pack archive is held to the sha1 its version publishes. `MrpackInstaller.installFromUrl` and both archive downloads in `ModrinthPackUpdater` (the target version and, for an instance with no file record, the installed one) fetched the `.mrpack` with no expected digest, while the index inside it is what pins every other file, so whoever served the archive chose the files and the hashes they were checked against together. `CataloguePackVersion` now carries `downloadSha1` and `downloadSize` from the primary file, `PackInstallCoordinator` refuses a version that publishes no digest, `installFromUrl` requires one and skips an already verified archive by digest, and `ModrinthClient.downloadTo` takes an optional sha1, which `ModInstaller` now passes for a mod jar too.
- A mod change that a held file blocked is carried out at the next launch. When the running game kept a jar open, `relabel` gave up on the rename and a sync or update gave up on dropping the stale variant, and both promised the change for "the next launch's sync", which does not exist: a switched-off mod whose active jar could not be moved or removed went on loading. The new `PendingVariants` records what each mod still owes in `.nexira-pending` beside the instance (a move between its two names, or a drop of a leftover while its replacement is present), keyed by the mod so a later decision replaces an earlier one and a success clears it. `LauncherController.preparePackLaunch` calls the new `IPackSyncService.settlePending` before the roster check, when the game that held the file has exited. The file is one of `INSTANCE_STATE_FILES`, so a snapshot and a rollback carry it with the files it describes.
- A rollback puts back the build, not the whole record it was taken with, and an update commits onto the record as it stands. `ApplyGuard`, `ApplyRecovery` and both manual rollbacks (`PackUpdateService`, `ModrinthPackUpdater`) wrote the snapshot's entire `PackInstance`, so undoing an update days later also undid playtime, last played, notes, the name and runtime settings changed since. The commits in `PackUpdateService` and `MrpackInstaller.update` wrote the record read when the update began, which lost playtime a game recorded while the update ran. The new `withBuildOf` names the build fields (version and pin, build key, installed and cached manifests) and merges optional toggles with the current choice winning, and every one of these writes goes through `IPackRepository.update`. A record deleted meanwhile is no longer brought back by the write.
- Deleting a pack is refused while its game runs or other work rewrites it, and the delete controls say why. `PackInstanceService.deleteCompletely` removed the tree and then the entry without asking: on Linux the files went from under a live game, which kept writing into a directory that no longer existed, on Windows the locked jars stopped it halfway, and an update running beside it kept writing into what was being removed. It now answers a `DeleteOutcome` (`Deleted`, `GameRunning`, `Busy`, `Incomplete`), runs under `InstanceMutationLock` and as the new `InstanceWork.Delete`, so a launch arriving meanwhile is refused with "Deleting…", and asks the running check again once that mark is up. `rememberPackDeleteBlock` puts the same answer beside the pack settings delete button and the Library card's menu item, which are disabled while it holds. The Library delete moved to the app scope, where leaving the screen cancelled it halfway through the tree.
- NeoForge resolves for Minecraft 1.20.1 and for the year-numbered releases. `ModernInstallerResolver` knew one of NeoForge's three version schemes: 1.20.1 is published as `net.neoforged:forge` with versions like `1.20.1-47.1.106`, and the year-numbered releases keep every part (`26.3` is `26.3.0.x`), so the first asked for a path that does not exist and the second for the prefix `3.0.` and found nothing, which made every release newer than 1.21.11 uninstallable with NeoForge. The new `neoforgeLine` names the artifact and prefix per era, the latest build now comes from the maven's version listing through `pickNeoForge` (the newest release, else the newest beta, which is all a new Minecraft version has for its first weeks, never a snapshot build), and the installer URL follows the artifact. A NeoForge 1.20.1 version typed without its `1.20.1-` prefix is accepted, and a Forge version typed with its Minecraft prefix is no longer prefixed twice. The URLs were checked against the live maven.
- A pack that named no loader version is pinned to the one it got. A blank version was stored as `""` and meant "the latest" on every launch: the loader moved under the pack with no notice, each new Forge or NeoForge promotion ran the headless installer again at the next Play, and the lookup needed the network, so the pack could not start offline. Every resolver now reports the version it resolved as `LoaderProfile.version`, which `ResolvedRuntime.loaderVersion` and `SpawnResult.Started.resolvedLoaderVersion` carry out. `LocalPackCreator`, `RetiredClientAdopter` (whose adoptions were all blank) and `ForeignInstanceImporter` record it at creation, and `LauncherController.pinLoaderVersion` records it on the first launch of a pack still blank, leaving a version the pack does name alone, including a legacy Forge build the resolver had to substitute. FTB discovery keeps the version after the dash in `modLoader` instead of cutting it off, and legacy Forge accepts a build written with its Minecraft prefix.
- A pack with a pinned loader relaunches with no network for every loader, not only vanilla and modern Forge or NeoForge. `FabricLikeResolver` fetched the loader profile on every launch, `ForgeLegacyResolver` read the maven metadata and downloaded the installer into a temp file it then deleted (every 1.12.2 pack, mirror ones included), and Cleanroom and lwjgl3ify downloaded their installer or profile each time, so a provisioned pack could not start offline. The new `LoaderSourceCache` keeps what was fetched for a named version under `loader-cache/`: the Fabric and Quilt profile, the legacy Forge installer beside the build it resolved to (so a build nobody published maps to its substitute without asking again), the Cleanroom installer and the lwjgl3ify profile. Only named versions are kept, since "the latest" is a question whose answer moves, and an entry that no longer reads is dropped and fetched again. Cleanroom and lwjgl3ify refuse a blank version with a sentence saying so, where it used to reach the URL as an empty segment and come back as a bare 404.
- A loader the launcher cannot install is refused instead of launching vanilla. `LoaderRegistry.resolverFor` answered null for vanilla and for an id it did not know alike, and `RuntimeProvisioner.ensureRuntime` read both as no loader, so a typo in the adopter's free-text loader field, a mirror manifest naming LiteLoader or an FTB import with an unrecognised loader started plain vanilla with every mod ignored and nothing said, against `PackLoader.Unknown`'s promise of an honest error. The new `LoaderRegistry.isVanilla` tells the two apart, and `ensureRuntime` fails naming the loader before anything is downloaded.
- A new instance's directory keeps its whole id, and a failed or cancelled install removes only a directory it made itself. Every installer bounded `"$name-$id"` to 96 characters as one string, so a long name pushed the id out, part or all of it, and every Cyrillic letter became an underscore, so two packs could reduce to one directory: the second install copied over the first, recorded its files as its own, and cancelling it deleted both. The new `instanceDirName` bounds the name and appends the id after, which `RetiredClientAdopter` already did, and the mirror, mrpack, CurseForge, create and foreign-import paths now share it. `PackInstallService` keeps only reservations that did not exist yet, and now cleans them up on a failure as well as a cancel, so a mistyped Minecraft version or an import that fails partway no longer leaves an unregistered directory, a copied game directory included, on every retry.
- Creating a local pack finds out it cannot work before it downloads anything, and offers every loader the launcher can install. `RuntimeProvisioner.ensureRuntime` downloaded the whole vanilla runtime, assets included, and only then resolved the loader, so Fabric on 1.12.2, Forge on a snapshot or a Minecraft version typo failed minutes later. It now reads the version json (which a relaunch has on disk) and resolves the loader first. The create dialog adds Cleanroom and lwjgl3ify, which had resolvers and no chip, and requires a version for them since their release pages have no latest to resolve, and both resolvers refuse any Minecraft but their own (1.12.2 and 1.7.10) instead of layering onto it. A version typed for one loader is cleared when the loader changes and is not stored on a vanilla pack, the field says whether empty means the latest or is not allowed, and each create has its own job key, where a second pack of the same name was a silent no-op after the dialog closed.
- The loader version in the create dialog is picked from a list. The field was bare text with nothing to choose from, while most of the data was already fetched and thrown away. `LoaderResolver.availableVersions` now answers per loader, newest first: Fabric and Quilt from their meta list (Quilt flags nothing stable, so nothing is marked a pre-release there), Forge for both eras from the maven metadata with the build its promotions recommend marked, NeoForge from the maven's listing for the Minecraft version's line with betas marked and snapshots left out, and Cleanroom and lwjgl3ify from their GitHub releases through the new `githubReleaseVersions`, which only this picker calls since GitHub rate-limits by address. `RuntimeProvisioner.availableLoaderVersions` is what the dialog asks, once the Minecraft version is one Mojang lists, and the menu marks the recommended build and pre-releases. The field stays free text, so a build the listing does not carry can still be named, and empty still means the latest.
- Deleting a pack removes what was kept about it outside its directory. The snapshots under `snapshots/<instanceDirName>/` and the apply-journal marker were left behind, and the snapshot prune only runs inside an apply, so up to three copies of a deleted pack's managed files stayed on disk for good, and a marker left by an interrupted apply had recovery chase a directory that no longer existed. `PackInstanceService.deleteCompletely` now removes both through the new `PackSnapshotService.deleteAll` and `ApplyJournal.complete`, and drops the size cache entry itself, which only the pack settings delete did (the Library delete, already moved to the app scope with the running-game guard, never did).
- Detaching a pack to Local drops its server binding. `PackInstanceService.detachToLocal` flipped only the origin, so the cached manifest's auth requirement stayed and the launch went on treating the pack as bound, sweeping `mods/` against the mirror's baseline, while the Content tab opened adding and removing mods because the origin was now Local: whatever the player added was deleted at the next Play. A pack whose contents are its owner's is not one a server session can be lent to, so the detach now clears `cachedManifest.authRequirement` as well, through `IPackRepository.update`, and the pack launches unbound. The detach description in all four languages says it ends updates and sign-in to the pack's server.
- A mod on disk under both of its names is one item, and neither toggling nor adding can put a stale or partial file under the name the game loads. `InstanceContentScanner` listed `x.jar` and `x.jar.disabled` as two entries with one identity, which Compose rejects as a duplicate key and the icon cache fought over as one slot with two sizes, and now lists only the loadable one. `InstanceContentManager.setEnabled` moved with `ATOMIC_MOVE`, which replaces its target, so enabling put the stale `.disabled` copy over the jar in use: enabling now leaves a jar already under the loadable name alone, and disabling moves that jar over the leftover. `addFiles` copied straight to the final name, so an interrupted copy published a truncated jar that the retry then failed on and a re-add skipped as present, and now stages the copy beside the target and moves it into place.
- Unpacking the natives either finishes or stops the launch, and a rebuild starts from an empty folder. `EnvironmentPreparer` logged a native jar that would not unpack, or was missing, and went on, and the validity check afterwards only asks for one file whose name carries `lwjgl`, so a partial folder passed and the game died inside the child JVM on the library that was not there, reported only as an exit code. Both now throw with the jar named, a folder the check still finds incomplete stops the launch, and a `.nexira-extracting` marker written for the length of the extraction keeps the next launch from trusting a folder whose last extraction died partway. A folder the player filled by hand, with no marker, is still left alone on an unbound launch. The rebuild a bound launch does cleared only the top level with `ClientFileHelper.cleanDirectory`, and the flatten step then moved a native from a subdirectory over what had just been unpacked, into the folder `java.library.path` names. It now removes the whole tree first.
- `LinuxUpdateApplicator` keeps the backup until the new version confirms it started. The shutdown hook started the new AppImage, waited two seconds and deleted the backup if the process was still alive, so a build that showed its window and died a few seconds later passed and left nothing to roll back to. The hook now swaps the binary, leaves `<exe>.update-pending` beside it and starts `WATCHDOG_SCRIPT`, a `/bin/sh` process that outlives the JVM. It waits for the old process to exit, which also ends a race in which the new launcher found the single-instance lock still held by the exiting one and quit, runs the new version, and when that version exits with the marker still there it puts the backup back and starts the old one with `NEXIRA_UPDATE_ROLLED_BACK` set. `IUpdateApplicator.confirmStarted` clears the marker and the backup. `AppShellContent` calls it beside `UiRecoverySignal.noteShellHealthy` once the shell has stayed up for `HEALTHY_SESSION_MS`, in the composition's own effect, so a crash reload in between cancels it. A target that predates the confirmation, such as an older release picked in the update manager, is kept once it has run for `PROBATION_GRACE_SECONDS`.
- The delta self-update engine keeps an interrupted apply resumable and its paths inside the install. Nothing calls it yet. The apply marker moves from `staging/` to `InstallLayout.applyMarker` in the install root, because `UpdateStager.stage` empties staging first and so destroyed the marker an interrupted apply resumes from, and both `stage` and `LauncherUpdater.update` refuse to start while one is pending. `LayoutApplier.recover` keeps the marker when the target manifest cannot be had, where dropping it left the layout half applied with the old manifest recorded. Remote paths go through `resolveWithinRoot` in the stager and the applier, and the applier checks every move and delete before it writes the marker. `ScheduledInstall` replaces the shutdown hook when an install is scheduled again instead of adding a second one, in all three applicators, and `UpdateDialog` disables its install button through the teardown delay with `DownloadState.Installing`.
- The relaunch after a second-factor code carries the session the code unlocked. `LauncherController.prepareScAuth` reached `authService.login` on that relaunch and relied on `AbstractCachingAuthProvider`'s thirty-second cache to hand the same session back, so a preparation that outlasted it (a roster sweep over a large `mods/`, a catch-up fetch) sent a real login. On SmartyCraft that mints a new uid, invalidates the session just unlocked and raises the demand again. `launchPackInstance` takes `sessionMintedForLaunch`, set only by the gate's relaunch in `LaunchDriver`, and a bound launch then carries that session as it is. A session minted at sign-in and held by the shell still signs in, so the experimental single-session mode stays the only reuse across launches.
- `TwoFactorPromptHost` clears the sticky two-factor flag only on a session that is not itself the product of a code. Its probe login could be answered from the provider's cache with the session a code had unlocked moments before, and it read that as the account having dropped its second factor, after which launches signed in silently until one met the demand again. `provesNoSecondFactor` makes the decision, and the cached session still goes to the waiting relaunch.
- One `SmartyCraftAuthProvider` serves both TLS channels. The `named("insecure")` provider behind `LoginPanel`'s connect-anyway retry kept its own session cache and pending second-factor map, so a code completed through it was unknown to the provider that the launch and `TwoFactorPromptHost` ask. The default `HttpClientProvider` already picks the bypass client per request once a grant exists, and `CertificateTrustGate.accept` records the grant before it runs the retry, so the retry now goes through the one provider and the insecure-qualified `AuthProvider`, `IServerProtocol` and `HttpClientProvider` are gone.
- An auto-login pass that answers after a manual sign-in leaves the manual one in place. The loop in `AppRoot` checked for a sign-in only before its network call, and the form stays usable while a pass is in flight, so the shell could end up on the stored account while the store had made the manual one active. The pass checks again once the answer is back. A Microsoft refresh token it rotated is still saved, since the stored one no longer works, through the new `AccountStore.saveAccount(makeActive = false)`. A rotation saved at startup never takes the active slot, whether or not a manual sign-in won the race: every start used to make the refreshed account the active one, so the active account was whichever one the boot happened to refresh, and now only an interactive sign-in moves it.
- `CredentialsManager` serialises every read-modify-write of `credentials.json` under one lock, and the migration of an older format reads the file again under it. One press of Play into the two-factor gate ran the gate's save and the launch's flag write side by side, and the slower one wrote back the list it had read before the other landed, which could re-arm the flag. `writeStringOwnerOnly` now writes through an owner-only temp file that `AtomicFiles.writeStringCreatedBy` renames into place, so a kill mid-write leaves the previous file instead of an empty one that reads as no accounts.
- The SmartyCraft uid lives in the vault under `<provider>:<account>:uid` beside the other secrets. It is the input to every signed action and sat in `credentials.json` in cleartext, so the file and a player name were enough to sign spawn, two-factor and skin upload while the token derived from the same uid was behind the keyring. `moveUidsIntoVault` moves the uid an older file still carries on the first read of a run, and leaves it in the file only when the vault refuses it. An older build reading the cleaned file has to sign in again before a signed action works.
- `MicrosoftSignInButton` saves the account on `Dispatchers.IO`. The call ran on the composition scope while the two beside it in the same block were already on IO, and it writes the keyring and the account file, so a Secret Service that asked for an unlock froze the window for the whole prompt at the moment device-code sign-in finished.
- An account is identified by its provider and its id together, in lookup and in removal. The id is the uuid or, without one, the player name, so two providers can share it. `accountFor` and `primarySession` found a record by provider and then rebuilt the session through `loadSession` by id alone, so the vault keys came from whichever record matched first and one provider could be handed another's token, password and refresh token. `removeAccount` deleted one provider's secrets and dropped every record with that id, which left the other provider's secrets in the keyring under keys no record named and `clear()` could not reach. Sessions are now built from the record found (`sessionOf`), and `loadSession` and `removeAccount` take the provider.
- The wardrobe auto-imports the server skin of the SmartyCraft account rather than of the session fronting the shell. The skin host is SmartyCraft's and answers by nickname, so a Microsoft face whose name also existed there downloaded a stranger's skin into the local library and marked it applied. `WardrobeSurface` keys the import on the SmartyCraft account, does nothing without one, and the preview shows that account's look, which is the one an apply changes.
- The sign-in a bound launch makes is written back to the stored account. Each SmartyCraft login mints a new uid and retires the old one, and `prepareScAuth` signs in before every bound launch, so after the first launch the stored uid was one the server had dropped and a skin upload signed with it. `ICredentialStore.refreshStored` updates an account that is already stored, keeps a password or refresh token the fresh session lacks, stores nothing the user chose not to remember and leaves the active slot alone. A failure there is logged and the launch goes on.
- `AbstractCachingAuthProvider` retries an auth round trip only when it never reached the server. `withRetry` retried a login or a code check up to three times on any transient failure, a read timeout and a reset mid-response included, which are requests the server may already have processed. A login on a two-factor account sends a code, so a rerun sent a second one and spent the first, and a rerun of a code check checked a spent code and called it wrong. `neverReachedServer` admits a refused or unestablished connection, a connect timeout and a name that did not resolve, and everything else reaches the caller as the same network-shaped `AuthException`.
- `SmartyCraftAuthProvider.generateGameToken` fails the sign-in when the session does not decrypt. It handed back the encrypted session it had failed to derive from, with no log line, and that value reached the game's command line as the access token, where the game died at the auth host with nothing pointing back at the sign-in. The failure is logged and raised as `AuthException(INTERNAL_ERROR)`, which does not read as a network error.
