The provider tests used a base64 string that was never a ciphertext and passed through that fallback, and now seal their sessions with the server's key.
- `CredentialsManager` opens a `credentials.json` written by a newer build read-only. It took any format at or above its own as current, so the next save stamped the older format over the newer file and dropped what this build does not know, where the layout graph, the pack registry and the theme store already refuse that write. The store now reads what it understands, makes every write a no-op, skips the uid move into the vault and records `ReadOnlyStore.Accounts` through `NewerBuildData`, so the shell's notice names the saved accounts (`readOnlyDataAccounts` in all four languages).
- The CLI launches with the requested provider's account or reports that none is stored. `resolveSession` fell back to the active account, so a launch asked for one provider ran under another identity, and `--user` renamed a stored account, which put a chosen name beside the real account's uuid and token on one command line. `CliArgs` now accepts `--user` only with the offline provider.
- `Redactor.registerSecret` returns a handle, counted per value, and the launch releases it once both of the game's streams have ended. The token handed to every game stayed in a process-global list for the life of the launcher, past sign-out and past removal of its account. Registration moves from `GameCommandBuilder` to `LauncherService.launchPackClient`, ahead of the logged command line, and `ProcessLogHandler.attach` takes an `onDrained` hook that fires after the last line of both streams, which can be later than the game's own exit when a child it started holds them. A failed spawn releases the handle at once.
- The account store is read and written off the UI thread. Every read goes to the OS keyring, one call per secret, each bounded only by the vault's timeout. The SmartyCraft and Microsoft profile sections and the wardrobe read it inside a `remember`, the face picker listed accounts there, and sign-out, account removal, the face choice and the face lookup after a sign-in ran on the click. Reads go through the new `rememberReadOffMain`, which keeps the previous answer on screen until the next one lands, and the click paths move their store work to `Dispatchers.IO`. A profile section draws nothing until the store has answered rather than a sign-in form for an account that is signed in, a second press of a section's sign-out while the first is at the store is ignored, and `AppRoot.doLogout` clears the store on IO before it signs the shell out.
- A full sign-out clears `offlinePlayerName` whichever identity was the face. It was cleared only when the offline identity fronted the shell, so with an online face the name stayed and `AutoLoginCoordinator`, finding no stored account, signed the offline identity back in at the next start. The offline mode switch is left as it is, since with no name and no account it signs nobody in.
- `SessionData.toString` masks the uid with the three other secrets. It printed the uid in full while the credential store keeps it in the vault as the input to every signed SmartyCraft action.
- The login form saves an account under its provider's own id through `saveAccount(session, authService.id)`. `AccountStore.save` inferred the provider from the session's shape, Microsoft when a refresh token was present, and the form was its only caller. The shim and `inferProviderId` are removed and the tests name the provider.
- `SmrtSyncService.plan` refuses a manifest entry whose address is not https and reports it unfetchable, like an entry nobody may serve. The bytes were pinned by sha1, but the request went wherever the manifest said, in plaintext or at a device on the player's own network. The `named("direct")` `HttpClientProvider` now builds on an OkHttp client with its own redirect following off (`withoutOwnRedirects`), so redirects reach Ktor's `HttpRedirect`, which refuses https to http. Handed a `preconfigured` client, Ktor's OkHttp engine uses it as given, and that client followed every redirect itself, the downgrade included. The `OkHttpClient` behind Coil keeps following them.
- `InstanceContentUpdater.applyOne` passes the update's published sha1 to `ModrinthClient.downloadTo`. It downloaded with no digest and hashed the scratch file afterwards, so a body that arrived whole but wrong failed the update where the transfer engine would have fetched it again, and the wrong bytes were written out first.
- The login form, the offline sign-in and the Microsoft sign-in no longer put the account or player name in the `ActionRing`. The ring pre-fills a crash report and goes into the diagnostic bundle, and `Redactor` has no rule that would catch a bare name.
- The April chaos close dialog's Close button can surrender. It was disabled until it had dodged the cursor eight times and the dodge counted hover interactions from its own interaction source, which a disabled clickable never emits, so it never enabled. Both ways out of the launcher go through that dialog in the April window, so on a machine without a working tray the launcher could not be quit. `AprilFoolsCloseDialog` keeps the button enabled and only draws it as refusing until it gives up.
- Every path built from a loader profile, an installer's output or a pack's version stays inside its root. `RuntimeProvisioner` bounded the vanilla library paths and joined a loader overlay's coordinate into a path with no check, and `MavenCoord.parse` validates no segment, so a `..` in a third-party profile wrote outside the shared libraries root. The overlay libraries, the installer-output files, the client jar, the cached version json and the asset index now go through `resolveWithinRoot`, and so does the path `ModernInstallerResolver.harvest` reads out of the installer's version json. The natives directory name is built from the pack's Minecraft version and emptied before it is filled, so `EnvironmentPreparer` and `GameCommandBuilder` resolve it within the instance, and `GameCommandBuilder` refuses a classpath entry that carries the path separator.
- A console text selection keeps following the pointer while the game prints. `LogCanvas` keyed both pointer handlers on the line models, a new value on every snapshot, so each appended line cancelled the gesture, and a drag cancelled in the edge band left the auto-scroll armed and extending the selection every frame. The handlers read the current lines, style and padding through `rememberUpdatedState`, and the drag handler disarms the edge scroll however its coroutine ends.
- A shell region cannot be moved onto another surface. The regions render other surfaces from code, so a loop can close through surfaces the nested-path guard does not see: the body region dropped into a container on home renders a centre that renders home. `moveWidget` takes `staysOnSurface` from the caller, and the editor sets it for kinds the descriptor marks non-removable.
- A slot that leaves the screen stops being a drop target. `DropTargetRegistry` never forgot a slot, and since the hit-test prefers the smallest rectangle, a stale one from a screen that had gone beat the live pane under it. The kernel signals through the new `LocalSlotBoundsWithdrawal` when a reporting slot leaves the composition, and empty-slot placeholders register and withdraw in a map of their own, so the placeholder's withdrawal leaves its slot's bounds alone. The unused `Modifier.slotBounds` is removed.
- Loading a preset keeps the state of widgets another saved preset still names. `WidgetStateGc` retained state for the live graph alone, so a preset load dropped every note and checklist the loaded preset did not carry, and loading the other preset back brought those widgets back empty. `PresetRepository.referencedInstanceIds` reads every instance id the saved presets name from the raw graph, and the collector keeps those too.
- A data-directory move that fails partway is retried, and a source that will not delete no longer loses the move. `DataDirMover` deleted the source before committing, so a delete that failed on files this process holds open on Windows left a half-deleted source as the data dir, and the two "retry next start" branches left a populated target that the next start refused for good. The target carries `.nexira-move-in-progress` while the copy runs and is copied again when a start finds it, a copy that fails or does not verify is removed, the new directory is committed before the source is deleted, and what survives of the source is recorded as `data-dir-stale-source` for the next start to delete. `.lock`, `.lock.pid` and `.show` are no longer copied: on Windows the lock is mandatory and reading it failed every copy.
- Resetting every surface is its own question. The reset dialog for one surface offered "Reset everything" as a second red button under a body saying the other surfaces are left untouched. It moves to the dismiss row and opens a confirmation of its own (`editorResetAllTitle`, `editorResetAllBody`).
- The shell root no longer recomposes per download chunk. `AppShellContent` collected the launch state whole, and it emits per chunk and per verified file, and every recomposition called `LayoutGraphRepository.observe` again, which hands out a new flow and restarted the layout collector. Consecutive preparing and downloading states are collapsed with `distinctUntilChanged`, and the layout flow is remembered.
- One failing message no longer stops the console. The `GameConsoleService` drain loop had no error handling and is the only consumer of an unbounded channel, so one throw ended the console for the process while producers kept enqueueing and a scroll-up waited for ever. `applySafely` confines a failure to its message and still answers one someone waits on, and `loadHistoryBefore` bounds its wait.
- A boot that fails before the shell starts can restart into recovery. The failed-boot panel offered the logs and quit, while the in-app way into recovery lives in a shell that had not started, so the same failure met every start. `ThresholdOverlay` gains a "restart into recovery" button that arms the one-shot marker and relaunches, and a line naming `--recovery` and `NEXIRA_RECOVERY`.
- Settings are changed as one step. `ISettingsService.updateSettings` runs a transform under `SettingsService`'s lock, and every read, change and save in the launcher uses it, the settings screen's merge included, so the slower of two writers no longer puts back a field the other had just changed. `BootstrapConf` wrote with `Files.writeString`, which truncates first, so a kill in between lost the data-dir override. It writes through `AtomicFiles` now and `update` is synchronized, and `LayoutManifest` and `LauncherHashCache` go through `AtomicFiles` too.
- The top bar's centre and right lanes are declared in the bundled layout. The bar rendered them and no layout carried them, so a drop on them was a write to an absent slot, which does nothing. `RenderedSlotsDeclaredTest` reads the sources for every slot rendered by name and fails when the bundled layout does not declare it.
- The databases open on first use, and the command line refuses to run beside the launcher. Both Xodus environments were opened while the dependency graph was built, and Xodus locks its directory for one process with no wait, so a second process on the same data dir failed inside `startKoin`. `XodusPackRepository` opens on first use, waits five seconds for a lock held briefly, and can hold it per operation (`holdOpen = false`, wired for the CLI through `transientPackRegistryModule`). The GUI reads it once on the boot thread, and a registry that cannot be opened comes up empty and read-only with the reason logged. `CacheFactory` opens on the first read or write and falls back to memory when it cannot. The CLI checks `SingleInstance.heldElsewhere` and exits with code 3 and a message when the launcher holds the data dir.
- A press the content list takes for a scroll no longer toggles the row it started on. `dragSelect` read the end of a press as a tap whenever it came before the hold threshold, and `waitForUpOrCancellation` answers null when the gesture is cancelled, which is what the list does when it claims the drag, so scrolling past a row during a running selection flipped that row. A cancelled press is now neither a tap nor a hold.
- A note or checklist too long to keep says so. `WidgetStateStore` refused an entry past its limit and kept the refusal to itself, so the widget went on showing text the next start would not have. `WidgetStateHost.refused` publishes the refused instances, `WidgetInstance.widgetStateRefused` reads one, and the notes and checklist widgets show `widgetStateTooLong` under the field.
- Stopping a launch during a JDK download is a cancellation. `JavaManagerService.downloadAndUnpack` caught the `CancellationException` with every other failure and walked on to the next mirror, which threw at once, so the stop was reported as every mirror failing.
- Stopping a launch while it signs in stops it. `LauncherController.prepareScAuth` caught the cancellation as a failed refresh and carried on offline to the next suspension point, and `settlePending`, `catchUpWithPack` and the snapshot write in `resolveOrFetchManifest` swallowed it in `runCatching`. They go through `runCatchingUnlessStopped`, which passes a cancellation on.
- The April chaos engine waits for the window's size and reads it as it is, and an event that fails gives its button back. `AprilFoolsEngine` took the size once at start, often before the window had one, and a failed event left its button out of the layout. `AprilFoolsState.isEscaped` counts the fleeing phase, so a fleeing button is drawn while it flees instead of vanishing.
- The wallpaper settings have one owner. `BackgroundManager` is a process singleton holding them as a `StateFlow` that every writer changes through `update(transform)`, the settings screen reads the shell's value instead of a copy taken when it opened, and `flush` writes behind the shell's 300ms debounce with a JVM shutdown hook for whatever is still owed. Three defects went with the copies. A change in the last 300ms before a quit was lost. A slider moved after the player changed the wallpaper's volume put the old volume back. And a video wallpaper whose transcode finished after its screen closed was never applied, because `BgImagePickerWidget` awaited the transcode on the screen's scope and read leaving as the user's cancel, after which the next change swept the finished transcode away. The pick now finishes on the app scope.
- With no pack played yet, quick launch offers the newest install. `rememberQuickLaunchTarget` took the most recently played and fell back to the most recently installed through two maxima, and the fallback never ran because the first is null only for an empty list. The home widgets share one order now, `RecentFirst`, so the quick-launch target is also the first row of the lists beside it.
- A cancelled swipe returns a notification card to its place. `NotificationCard` handled the end of a horizontal drag and not its cancellation, so a drag taken by another gesture left the card parked half off screen and faded until its group was dismissed. The gesture also reads the current dismiss callback through `rememberUpdatedState`.
- A clickable `NxRow` lays out under an unbounded width. `bleedHorizontally` added its bleed to `constraints.maxWidth`, which overflows when the width is unbounded and leaves a negative maximum the constraint constructor throws on, so a clickable row in a horizontal scroller took the window down. An unbounded width stays unbounded.
- Hovering a row no longer recomposes it every animation frame. `NxRow` and `NxNavRow` read their animated hover alpha in composition. `softHoverAlpha` hands back the `State` and `drawSoftHover` reads it in the draw phase, as `NxSurface` already does.
- The mirror and Modrinth clients run their metadata reads on IO, request and decode both. `SmrtPackClient.getJson` and `ModrinthClient`'s `getJson`, `getJsonOrNull` and `postJson` ran on the caller's dispatcher, and the pack settings sections and the breadcrumb call them from a composition, so a full manifest, or a catalogue title fanning out into a summary, a manifest and a build list, was decoded on the UI thread. `PackVersionSection` asks the mirror only about a mirror pack, where it spent two requests on 404s for a Modrinth instance, and its reads and `PackContentSection`'s fetch pass a cancellation on instead of writing it as offline over the loading state of the fetch that replaced them.
- Clearing the mimic-version field or the alternate news field is saved. `AdvancedSection` compared each debounced field with the settings as the screen opened, so once a value had been saved, clearing the field matched that opening value again and the clear was never written. Each field compares with what it last saved.
- A large text file previews a row per line. `FileBrowserPane` read up to 256 KiB off the UI thread and then laid all of it out as one `Text`. `TextPreview` is a `LazyColumn` over `previewLines`, which splits a line past 4096 characters so a minified file is not one huge row either.
- The content tab writes its state on the UI thread only. `ContentTabState.rescan` runs on the app scope after a change to the disk and assigned the scan, the pack's placed keys and the cached icons from whichever IO thread it resumed on, and so did the bulk delete clearing the selection and `checkUpdates` when a finished batch starts it. The tab already documents a crash from this for its icon cache. All of these writes go through `Dispatchers.Main`.
- A customization reset is not written back by the console or wallpaper store. `RecoveryIo.resetCustomization` deletes `console.json` and `background.json` while the launcher runs, and `ConsoleSettingsStore` and `BackgroundManager` wrote their copies back through the debounce or the shutdown hook without checking `RecoveryIo.stateWasReset`, which the layout and widget-state stores honour. Both skip the write once it is set.
- The second-instance signal is polled off the UI thread. The `.show` watcher in `AppShellContent` checked for the file and deleted it on the composition thread twice a second, which is a stall per tick on a network-mounted or sleeping data directory. The check and the delete run on IO.
- The drag ghost is a picture of the widget, not a second copy of it. `EditableWidgetChrome` handed `DragController` a ghost that composed the widget's content again, so two live copies ran under one instance id for the length of a drag, and the ghost's `provideService` took over the source's registration and withdrew it on the drop. The source records its drawing into a `GraphicsLayer` and `RecordedGhost` draws that layer, which needs no second composition and no carried-over surface locals. `DragGhostLiveCopyTest` drags a widget off screen and counts the copies alive at once.
- One unreadable prop falls back alone. `decodeProps` fell back to the defaults whole when any field failed, so one bad value in the hand-editable layout file reset every prop on the widget, while the prop panel read the raw record and showed values the widget was not using. `readableProps` drops only the fields that fail on their own, `decodeProps` decodes through it, and `WidgetPropPanel` reads through it too and marks a row whose saved value could not be read (`editorPropUnreadable` in all four languages).
- A prop change is applied to the props as they stand. The prop panel and `ShellRightRegion`'s collapse toggle built a whole props object from what their composition last saw and handed it to a write that lands later, so a slider drag and a collapse close together put back what the other had changed. `EditModeController.updatePropsFrom` applies a transform to the stored props inside the serialized update, and each field gets its own undo key.
- A Long, Short or Byte prop is edited as a number. `PropFieldRow` routed only Int to the numeric controls, so another whole-number width fell through to the free-text row and was written back as a string the decoder refuses. `SerialKind.isWhole` covers all four. An Int or floating prop with no declared range used to fall through to the free-text row too, so a typed word failed the props decode and reset the widget to its defaults. The numeric row refuses a keystroke `isNumeric` rejects, and takes ASCII digits only, because `Char.isDigit` accepts Arabic-Indic digits that `toDoubleOrNull` will not parse.
- `InstanceSizeService` walks an instance once at a time and a finished walk removes only its own entry. The check for a running walk and the registration of a new one were separate steps, so two callers on different threads could both walk a multi-gigabyte tree, and the cleanup removed by key, which could take out a walk started after it. The walk is claimed in one `compute` before it starts and removed with `remove(id, job)`.
- The SmartyCraft join checks are made against what the launch can do. The warning that a bound join has nothing to steer it read the two settings, so an agent switched on but not extracted stayed quiet, and the authlib swap was demanded on the pack's binding alone, so an offline launch, whose session had already lost its token and cannot join, was refused for want of a patched authlib. `LauncherService.joinsBoundServer` asks whether the session carries a token, the binding and the agent apply only when it does, and `joinMechanismWarning` reads the agent as produced.
- A widget module whose registry cannot list its widgets is refused by name. `WidgetModuleLoader` guarded instantiating a module's registry but not listing it, and the list was first read while building the composite registry the shell resolves from, so a throwing registry failed that singleton and the shell restarted into the same jar until safe mode. The loader lists each registry once inside its guard and refuses one that cannot, with the failure as the reason in the module list.
- A managed runtime left aside by an unfinished install is put back, not deleted. `JavaManagerService.installUnpacked` renames the working copy to `.previous` and the new one into place, and a crash between the two left the working copy aside with nothing at the target, which the next attempt deleted before it had even fetched an archive. `recoverInterruptedSwap` moves it back, at lookup and at the start of an install.
- Another launcher's runtime files are taken only where they match the manifest. `ForeignInstanceImporter` hard-linked a vanilla-layout `.minecraft`'s assets, libraries and client jar into the shared roots on an existence check, and the provisioner then skips a present file by size for every pack, so a foreign file of the right size was never verified again, and a library sits at a maven coordinate anything can have written to. The importer now hands `RuntimeProvisioner.ensureRuntime` a `RuntimeSeed`, and the provisioner takes a file from it only when its sha1 is the one the Mojang manifest or the loader profile gives for that path.
- A modded vanilla-launcher profile imports with its real game version and loader. `MinecraftLauncherSource` took `lastVersionId` as the Minecraft version, which for a modded profile names the loader's version folder, and the runtime step could not resolve it. `vanillaProfileVersion` reads `inheritsFrom` and the loader and its version from the folder's libraries or launch arguments, accepts a folder it cannot read only when its name is a plain Minecraft version, and refuses a modded one whose loader it cannot name. `ForeignInstanceImporter` refuses a named loader with no version, which would have resolved to that loader's newest build instead of the one the pack was assembled on.
- A heap typed into a pack's JVM arguments is the one the game gets. `GameCommandBuilder` appended its own `-Xms`/`-Xmx` after the typed arguments and the JVM takes the last occurrence. `JvmHeapArgs` tells a typed maximum (`-Xmx`, `MaxHeapSize`, `MaxRAM`, `MaxRAMPercentage`, `MaxRAMFraction`) from a typed minimum. A maximum suppresses the builder's heap flags. A minimum alone drops only the builder's `-Xms` and keeps its `-Xmx`, raised to the typed minimum when that is larger, since dropping the ceiling too handed the game the JVM default of a quarter of the machine. The launch console and the pack's memory section say the setting does not apply only for a typed maximum.
- `XodusPackRepository.stampSchema` brings the stored schema up to this build's on every writable open. The stamp was written only inside the one-time legacy migration, so a database from before a bump kept the old number and the newer-build guard never fired for it.
- The April chaos engine stops when its window ends instead of tilting the window until exit, puts an escaped button back before dropping it, counts intensity from the first of April with a forced off-season run as day one, and sizes an escaped clone through the display density rather than taking its pixel size as dp. `AprilFoolsProgress` reads a finished download as full.
- The example pixel player skips a file that will not open, going round the folder at most once, instead of halting the queue on it.
- `SkinView3D` redraws a snapped pose: its frame hook reads `animationRevision`, which a snap bumps while the pose clock stays put. `SkinViewSnapRedrawTest` waits for the off-thread render to land and then to change.
- The authlib agent prints a stderr line when it cannot register its transformer or rewrite a class, where both used to fail silently and leave the server refusing the join with nothing said on the player's machine.
- Screens: Browse keys its snapshot effect on the retry as well, the content tab's file picker runs on the screen's scope, the versions list keys its expanded runs on the installed build, the mouse thumb buttons answer only for the main window through `MouseEvent.isFrom`, and the offline sign-in catches a failed write and clears its loading flag.
- `LibTrayController` publishes its fields across threads and installs and removes the tray under one lock with a generation counter, so a shutdown during the seconds-long init no longer leaves an icon nothing can remove. `ProfilerAgent` keeps its heap pool names in a concurrent set read by the GC notification thread. The German and Russian catalogues build their notification time formatter once.
- Reduce motion reaches the dark-mode reveal, the row hover overlay and the 3D skin view, which were documented as motion-aware and read nothing. `Symbol` remembers its variation settings and font family per axis value instead of rebuilding them per composition. The unused `FavoriteBorder` icon is dropped and the wallpaper tint goes through the shared colour parser.
- Console: `LaunchDriver` stops building strings for a notification that no longer exists, `SessionRegistry` registers and unregisters under one lock so an uptime ticker cannot be orphaned, `GameConsoleService` finds a slot's line by identity, and `UpdateDialog` rethrows a cancellation.
- Rendering: `BackgroundOptimizer.scaleRgba` owns each native object in a try from the moment it exists, `SvgImageDecoder` scales an oversized icon by one factor instead of squashing it, and `Scene3DView`'s renderer compares the output size and supersampling factor before reusing its scratch buffers.
- Widget kernel: `kotlinEscape` escapes dollar signs, line breaks, carriage returns and tabs, `WidgetModuleLoader` refuses a module whose default props do not decode, whose props class or plane cannot be loaded (guarded against linkage errors, which used to fail every start), or that declares a plane beside `drawsOwnSurface`, the validator makes slots on an instanceless widget and that plane pair build errors, and the palette no longer copies a widget's declared plane onto the new instance.
- `LauncherController` arms the session content guard before writing `lastPlayed`, and stops the game and ends the launch as `LaunchError.Internal("session-guard")` both when arming throws and when the guard's own coroutine fails while it watches, which before only reached the scope's handler. A launch that fails after the spawn for any reason but a plain cancellation ends the game, where an `Error` used to forget the handle with the game still running. A first launch records the loader version it resolved only if the pack still names the same loader. `deleteTree` retries a refused delete through the DOS read-only flag on Windows only, and `RetiredClientAdopter` turns that off for its hardlinked tree. `JavaManagerService`, `DataDirMover` and `RetiredClientAdopter` use it instead of their own copies. A pack registry schema stamp that cannot be written no longer empties the library for the session.
- Browse shows a pack published on the mirror while it is open. `SmrtPackClient.listPacks` takes `forceRefresh` like its siblings and `packsStream` gives the stale-then-fresh listing. `MirrorPackCatalogue` is no longer wrapped in `CachedPackCatalogue`, whose cache took the listing cache's stale answer as fresh, and its `searchStream` polls the mirror every minute while collected, a refresh that fails behind the stored list included. `IPackCatalogueService.paged` is false for the mirror, so Browse treats its answer as the whole list and replaces it in place. The stored listing is kept for thirty days. The versions screen carries the user's pick over a refresh as the refreshed object, so its notes follow the language.
- A conflict in a pack's optional content is shown, not prevented. `OptionalContentRules.problems` names, for each enabled mod, the enabled mods it cannot run beside and the hard requirements that are off, and on a mod that is off, who needs it, so a required mod's missing library shows on the library's row in the pack settings too. The Content tab tints such a row light red with the other mod's name and the pack settings show the reason with a warning mark. Nothing is refused or switched off behind the player: `applyToggle` still turns on what a mod needs and off its same-role and incompatible optionals, as the hint says, never switches a required mod or one in the enabled mod's own requirement closure off, and never cascades on disable. Enabling a mod used to turn a required one off in the toggle state, which the sync then forced back on, so the screen and the folder disagreed. References in `requires` and `incompatible_with` match by filename or by stable key, a key naming every entry that carries it, and are resolved once per manifest through an index.
- A library older than the build a mod pinned is shown with its update offered. Modrinth has no version ranges, so a pinned `version_id` is the only statement of which build a dependency has to be. `pinnedRequirements` picks the pins and `isBehind` compares by publish date. `InstanceContentUpdater.check` reads the pins out of the installed versions it already fetches, the pack's own mods included through `context`, and returns each enabled file behind one as `PinBehind`. A file the player owns gets the pinned build as its update wherever the check found none at least that new, a pin that could not be looked up leaves the check incomplete and uncached, and a warning disappears with the mod that needed the newer build. The Content tab tints the row and names the mod that needs the newer build, and checks again after the mod browser closes. `ModInstaller` leaves a present dependency as it is. The download-and-replace step is the shared `swapIn`.
- Guards written once with a reason beside them now hold at the sibling sites that lacked them. The launch path: `LauncherController.pinLoaderVersion` is no longer a suspend point inside the spawn and writes through the app scope, so a Stop landing there can no longer leave a running game nobody owns. `TwoFactorPromptHost` passes a cancellation on before its generic catch, as `LauncherController` does, so a second Play no longer has the first prompt's cancellation wipe the new request, and its account saves sit inside the guarded block with `makeActive = false`, as `refreshStored` promises. `JavaManagerService` provisions under a per-directory lock, as `ModernInstallerResolver` installs, so two first launches needing the same Java no longer swap the directory under each other. `LauncherService` puts `fml.toml` back when `buildPackCommand` throws, not only when the spawn does. `PackDetailState.dismissNotes` changes the one field on the record as it stands through `repo.update`.
- The pack and content paths, the same way. Mod installs from `VersionsPane`, `ModDetailScreen`, `ModVersionScreen` and the content browser run on the app scope, as `ContentTabState` writes do, so leaving the screen no longer cancels an install between the jar and its required dependencies. `ModInstaller.fetch` and `InstanceContentManager.replace` resolve a server-named file name through `resolveWithinRoot`, as the mirror sync and the mrpack installer do. The content tab's update, update-all and version switch go through the running-pack guard. `CurseForgeZipInstaller` reserves the instance directory before creating it, as the other two installers do, so a cancelled or failed import is cleaned up. `PackSnapshotService` deletes snapshots with `clearReadOnly = false`, as `RetiredClientAdopter` does for hard-linked trees. The content updater's batch rethrows a cancellation. `ModDetailState` carries the install scope, handed the app scope by the screens and its own by a render sheet, so `Header`, `VersionsPane` and `ModVersionBody` no longer look the scope up and the mod page sheets render without an application.
- The secrets path. The command-line build prints the game's output and its own errors through `Redactor`, as the console does, so a launch no longer prints the access token. `IssueReporter` folds the home directory in the crash title and body with `DiagnosticBundle.foldHome`, a whole-path-segment `abbreviateHome` over free text, as the bundle body already kept the account name out of the URL. `NotificationCenter.push` redacts the body on the way in, as `ActivityRegistry` does, which also covers the archive on disk. `AutoLoginCoordinator.Resolution.Success.signedIn` marks a session minted by a sign-in, and `AppShell` writes it back with `refreshStored`, as a launch that signs in does. `CredentialsManager` keeps the v5 flat keys and the legacy keyring when the vault refuses the migrated secrets, as `moveUidsIntoVault` keeps the uid, and `storeSecrets` reports whether every store took.
- The caches. `VideoCacheFiles` is the one rule both writers of `video-cache` use for what is still downloading: the transfer engine's staging file and journal, and yt-dlp's `.part-FragN` fragments, `.ytdl` index and `.temp`. `YtDlpService` accepts only `<hash>.<ext>` as a finished fetch, so a fragmented download is no longer served as the video, and eviction no longer deletes a paused transfer's journal. Its kill lists the descendants first and ends them after the parent, as `ModernInstallerResolver` does, since the release builds run the program as a child of a one-file launcher. `PackArtResolver` caches only answers and rethrows a cancellation, as `ModIconResolver` does.
- `MacUpdateApplicator.INSTALL_SCRIPT` copies the new bundle to `$BUNDLE.new`, steps the installed one aside only once the copy is whole and swaps by rename, starting the installed version again on any failure before the swap, as `LinuxUpdateApplicator` and `JavaManagerService` order theirs. The script is a constant and runs under bash in `MacUpdateApplicatorTest` with stand-ins for the macOS tools. The update goes in under the bundle's own name.
- The layout editor. `EditModeController.loadArrangement` puts a saved preset in place with `withScreensFrom` and `ScreenLinks.ensureLinks`, as `resetAll` does, and both it and `rearrange` (the built-in presets) are recorded in the undo history. `WidgetStateGc` skips a layout `LayoutGraphRepository.isReadOnly` reports, so a file left as it was found no longer has its widgets' state swept. A prop of the wrong shape is read as absent in `ShellCenterRegion`, `ScreenLinks.target` and three `JsonMigrations` steps, as the migration's own accessors do. Every drag and resize gesture in `EditableWidgetChrome` and `dragSource` ends in a `finally`, so a gesture cut off by Esc or Ctrl+E no longer leaves the ghost and the hidden cursor up. The right rail's swipe writes the collapsed value it ends on, the notification history row reads its dismissal through `rememberUpdatedState`, both go back to rest on cancel, and the history's 12-hour marker comes from the locale. `PresetManagerPanel` catches storage failures and says so in the dialog.
- Settings and screens. `SettingsService.updateSettings` reads `settings.json` again when it no longer holds what the service last read or wrote, so a module switched off from the recovery surface is no longer switched back on by the next ordinary write. The wardrobe's apply runs on the app scope, as the profile upload does. The video scrubber seeks inexactly on release and on replay, as every wallpaper seek does, and `MediaSlider` reads its callbacks through `rememberUpdatedState`. `AdvancedSection` writes its two debounced fields on dispose, as `PackSettingsSheet` does. `CompactNewsFeed` keys its state on the feed. The pack version check and the build diff pass a cancellation on, as `bestEffort` does. Library imports are keyed on the absolute path. The diagnostic bundle's state lives in `BundleState` for the process. `UpdateDialog` sets the download state before launching it. The profile skin upload takes one file at a time. The timeline player seeks once on release through `seekAlong`. `WallpaperSession` writes from a poll only while its player is attached, under the lock a hand-over takes. `NxColorField` takes the record's value only while unfocused.
- `NxParticleField` sleeps a 33 ms floor between frames and stops while the window is unfocused, as `SkinView3D` does.
- `METADATA_TIMEOUT_MS` and `metadataTimeout()` in `hivens.core.net` bound every metadata read to twenty seconds: the loader version pickers (`FabricLikeResolver`, `ForgeLegacyResolver`, `LiteLoaderResolver`, `Lwjgl3ifyResolver`, `githubReleaseVersions`), `RuntimeProvisioner`'s version manifest and version json, both news feeds and the launcher's own update checks. `ModrinthClient` and `SmrtPackClient` use the shared helper instead of their own copies.
- `DataDirMigration` marks its target with `.nexira-migration-in-progress` while it copies and takes an unfinished copy up again, and leaves the legacy directory's `.lock`, `.lock.pid` and `.show` behind, as `DataDirMover` does.
- `rememberPackArt` re-applies its starting value on any key change. `ActivityDriver` forgets the phase of an install or content batch its source dropped, as `InstallDriver` does, and keeps its memory in a concurrent map. `CleanroomResolver.hostLibraries` applies the version json's library rules, as `ModernInstallerResolver` does, and `libraryRulesAllow` matches an os name carrying `-arm64` on an arm64 host. `LoginPanel.doLogin` returns while a sign-in is in flight. `SkinLibrary.addUnique` looks and adds under one hold of its lock.
- A layout file that fails to parse, fails to read or holds one instance id twice opens read-only under a third `ReadOnlyReason`, `Damaged`, with its own notice, as the old-format branch of `LayoutGraphRepository.load` already did. Writable, the first edit of the session wrote the bundled default over it.
- `SystemNotifier` has the `INITIALIZING` state, lifecycle lock and generation `LibTrayController` has, and closes a notifier whose creation finished after a shutdown. On a shell restart after a crash, a shutdown landing while the daemon was being reached was lost, or a second init opened another connection over the first, and either way a D-Bus connection and its subscription stayed open for the life of the process.
- `ProcessLaunchHandle.terminate` lists the game's descendants before any signal and gives those that outlive it the same polite signal and grace, then the forced one. They were listed only on the escalation, after the game had gone, so a game that took SIGTERM left what it had started running, and a wrapper script that does not exec left the game itself.
- A critical toast's pulse redraws its stripe and nothing else. `NotificationCard` read the pulse's animated alpha in its composable body, so the whole card recomposed every frame for as long as a critical toast stood, which is until it is dismissed. `criticalPulse` hands back the `State`, and the stripe reads it in `drawBehind` and mixes between the two ends of the wash taken in composition, as `NxSurface` and `NxRow` hold their animated values.
- The example `PixelPlayer` seeks and tears down under its gate, as its own KDoc says every compound operation must. The teardown after the last view detached read the view count off the engine dispatcher and closed outside the gate, so a view remounting at the end of the grace could open a track between the check and the close, and a seek could land on a player a step was about to drop.
- A launch stopped while it prepares writes nothing after the stop. `abort` reopens the launcher at once, while the cancelled launch runs on to its next suspension point, and a stage or an error it set in that stretch went over Idle or over the launch started since: an error there reads as Play, so another launch could be accepted while that one went on to spawn a game the controller would not track. Every write the prepare and spawn steps make to `_state` now goes through the launch's `Attempt`, which lands it under `launchLock` only while `ownsController` holds and the launch's abort token is clear.
- An update or a rollback that waited out a delete writes nothing. `PackAutoUpdateService` checks a pack, then waits for its `InstanceMutationLock`, and `PackInstanceService.deleteCompletely` can hold that lock first. Under it `PackUpdateService` and `ModrinthPackUpdater` fell back to the record read before the wait, so the apply wrote the whole build back into the directory the delete had removed, with no record left to show it in the Library. Both refuse with `InstanceRemovedException` once the record is gone, for an apply and for a rollback, and the pass drops the status of a pack removed while it ran instead of reporting it as failed.
- The interface's own saves leave the event thread. The theme flip, the theme mode and theme pickers, the wallpaper colours, the language, the customization, the settings screen, the remember-me box and the do-not-disturb switch each called `SettingsService.updateSettings`, `ThemeManager.save` or `CustomizationManager.save` on the click, and each is an `AtomicFiles` replace with two fsyncs, so the window stood still for as long as the disk took. They go through `PreferenceWriter`, one daemon thread that takes them in the order they were asked for, so two flips of one switch cannot land the other way round, and a shutdown hook drains what is still queued. `SettingsScreen.save` takes a read-only snapshot of the form at the click and merges inside it on the writer's thread.
- A pack registry write cancelled after it changed memory still reaches the disk. `XodusPackRepository.store` updated the in-memory state and then dispatched the durable write to IO, and a cancellation landing on that dispatch threw before the write and before the revert, so the edit stayed on screen and was gone after a restart. The pack settings save from an effect the next edit cancels, which is the caller that could hit it. The write and the delete run under `NonCancellable` once memory has changed.
- The pack classpath keeps the order the loader declared. `GameCommandBuilder` hoisted every bootstrap jar (launchwrapper, asm, bootstraplauncher, foundation) to the head of `-cp`, which also lifted them over loader jars declared ahead of them, and classpath order decides which duplicate root resource wins. Three jars in a 1.12.2 launch carry a `log4j2.xml`. Cleanroom got foundation's, whose `%rgbFormat` converter nothing registers, so log4j read `%r` as uptime, dropped the message and printed no newline because the pattern's `%n` sits inside that converter. Legacy Forge got the vanilla client's, which has neither the logger field nor the `forge.logging.*` levels. Only the client jar moves now, to the slot right after the last jar `isBootstrapJar` matches, and every library keeps its declared position.
- `HtmlRender` draws a run of bare `<br>` between two blocks as the vertical space it stands for. The breaks reached the inline branch as a string of newlines that the blank-text guard refused to draw, so a description spaced that way stacked its images flush. A line after a hard break no longer starts with a space: Markdown writes the break as a backslash and a newline, and Jsoup collapses that newline to one space without removing it, so every continuation line sat a few pixels right of the one above.
- `DefaultCache` stores a loaded value and completes before it releases the leader. It used to release first, so a caller arriving in that window started a second load of the same key. A failed load releases first so the next caller elects itself, and a load cancelled with its caller is told apart from one that failed. `CacheConfig.staleMode` adds `StaleMode.FallbackOnFailure`, which fetches past the TTL and keeps the old value only when the fetch fails, and the Modrinth project cache uses it, because `Eager` handed a one-shot `get` the stale value for the whole visit. `XodusDiskStore.describe` unwinds its path after recursing, so a record with two lists no longer fingerprints the second under the first one's name. `ModIconResolver` throws on a failure instead of returning null, which the caller had cached as the jar having no icon for good, and rethrows a cancellation. `ModrinthClient.versionByHash` answers null only for a 404, and `newestMatchingVersion` no longer falls back to the newest build for another loader. `InstanceContentScanner` collects every manifest a jar declares rather than the first and drops TOML comment lines before reading keys, and the scan cache format moves up with it.
- The stores holding what the user authored stop rewriting what they could not read. `ThemeManager` stamps `schema_version`, and a theme file from a newer build opens read-only under `ReadOnlyStore.Theme`. A layout older than the surface schema closes the store for writing, as the newer-build branch already did, where the first edit of the session used to persist the bundled default over the file it had declined to read. `ReadOnlyReason` (`NewerBuild`, `UnreadableFormat`) tells the two cases apart in the notice, since one is fixed by updating and the other by going back. `scaleMode`, `loopMode` and `navSelectionStyle` are stored as strings parsed with a fallback (`parseScaleMode`, `parseLoopMode`, `parseNavSelectionStyle`) and carried through verbatim, with the serialised form unchanged, so a constant from a later release survives a build that cannot read it. The prop panel stores only what differs from the widget's declaration through `propsDelta`, since writing the whole effective object froze every field at that release's default. A number or text field takes the record's value only while it is not focused. The recovery surface's appearance reset now includes `customization.json`.
- The failed-boot overlay no longer draws the progress frame under its error. The frame is also snapped to whole device pixels: centring put its left edge on a half pixel on an odd-width window, which antialiased each pixel-art band into two soft ones.
- A placed widget occupies what it draws. The placement branch of `SlotRenderer` applied a claim with `Modifier.size`, so a token placed at 412 reported 412 wide and painted 96, and the editor frame, the printed size and the room held against a neighbour were all the claim rather than the pixels. The claim is now a maximum per axis, as the flow branch already read it. `playerObject` stops taking the whole width to centre itself, so in a flow slot an object player now sits at the start. The grab-margin clamp measures what was drawn and falls back to the claim only before the first measurement. `AdaptiveWidget` scaled whenever both axes arrived bounded, and a slot that fills its surface hands each child the rest of the pane, so a clock dropped on Home drew its 140dp dial across 2974 points. `LocalWidgetFootprintDp` now carries the widget's own stored size, zero on an axis nobody named, and an adaptive widget reads that instead of its constraints and draws at its declared reference when it has no footprint.
- The editor's overlays sit beside the content pane that is actually laid out. The pill, the palette and the property panels were held off the rails by two constants, 65dp of left rail and the right rail's default width, and both are props, so a folded or widened rail left the overlays a rail short or on top of it. The centre region reports its rectangle through `reportsContentPane` into `ShellChromeBounds` (`LocalShellChromeBounds`), and the overlays take their side gaps from it, sideways only. The rectangle is read in a composable of its own, so a resize or a rail swipe no longer recomposes the editor host every frame. Once the pane is narrower than a floating panel, the gaps are given back continuously, end first, rather than dropped at a threshold, which used to throw the panel across the window on every pixel of a width slider. A zero rail width now means each rail's own default (`NAV_RAIL_DEFAULT_WIDTH`, `RAIL_DEFAULT_WIDTH`), and the label reads automatic: on the nav rail, zero was a weight of a row measured at its contents, so it swallowed the window, including the panel holding the control. `ContentPaneReportTest` composes the report, provide and read chain off screen and checks that the rectangle is in pixels.
- The palette and surface property panel headers drag again. Both read `positionChange()` after consuming the change, which then reports zero, so the panel moved by nothing. The delta is read first. The palette header drags on the primary button only, so the secondary-button resize it documents is reachable.
- A widget that scrolls or lists lazily no longer takes the shell down in a slot with no height. Compose throws when such a component is measured against an unbounded axis, and the editor lets any widget into any slot. `RenderWidget` fills an unbounded axis from the widget's declared maximum (`unboundedAxisCeiling`), in the one funnel every widget passes through, and leaves a bounded axis, and an undeclared widget outside a scrolling slot, as they were. `AboutCreditsWidget`, `HomeNewHero`, `HomeNewRecent`, `LibraryBody`, `NotificationHistoryWidget`, `ProfileNavWidget` and both theme picker widgets declare their sizes. `UnboundedSlotTest` pins the fill and its absence.
- A collapsed right rail reserves no width in edit mode. It drew a 22dp strip so its Tune stayed hoverable, which made the editing canvas narrower than the live one, so a widget at the right edge overflowed a boundary that existed only while editing. The rail reopens with Ctrl+N, which fires in the editor. The left rail keeps its strip, since it has no reopen chord.
- A player's measure seeks once, on release. `PlaybackScrubber`, `seekAlong` (the strip and the column) and `seekByAngle` (the ring) sought on the press, again on the lift and once per frame of a drag. They now only track where the gesture would land and seek when the pointer lifts.
- `AudioPlayer.open` keeps a playing transport playing. An open was always silent, from when the only open was a picker press, so changing the track while one sounded stopped the music. It reads whether the state was `Playing` before the load and passes that on as `autoplay`.
- A tag written in a legacy CJK encoding but declared ISO-8859-1 shows as text instead of Latin-1 noise. `trackInfoFrom` runs each tag through `repairMojibake`, which re-encodes the string to its Latin-1 bytes and tries strict UTF-8, Shift_JIS and GBK decoders, accepting a candidate only when it carries CJK and has shed the Latin-1 supplement characters. A string with any character above U+00FF, or one that is mostly ASCII, is left alone. Both runtime module lists in `client-ui/build.gradle.kts` gain `jdk.charsets`, without which the packaged runtime has no Shift_JIS or GBK.
- `WallpaperSession.seek` lands on the nearest keyframe (`exact = false`). An exact seek on a GPU-decoded wallpaper failed the frame download intermittently, on 720p60 H.264 over VAAPI somewhere between the third and the hundredth press, and skinema ends a hardware-decoded player on that failure, so the wallpaper went blank for the rest of the session. The same seeks asked inexactly ran 1920 times clean. Every player widget and the media session reach the wallpaper through this call, and `stop` seeks to zero inexactly too. The cost is a landing up to one keyframe interval early, with sound and picture re-anchored together.
- FML's early loading screen is switched where the loader reads it, per pack, and only for the length of one launch. FML holds a lock across a vsync buffer swap and gives Minecraft one second to take the window over, and on Wayland a window on a workspace nobody is looking at gets no frame callbacks, so the handoff failed with "trouble handing off the window". `GameCommandBuilder` passed `-Dfml.earlyprogresswindow=false`, which only Forge 1.13 to 1.19 reads, while NeoForge and Forge 1.20+ read `earlyWindowControl` from `config/fml.toml` and no property. `InstanceRuntime.earlyLoadingScreen` holds the pack's choice: unset means off on a Wayland session and the pack's own value elsewhere, and a choice is enforced both ways (`EarlyLoadingScreen.enforced`). When the resolved runtime carries the early display library (`configurableIn`), `LauncherService` writes that one value through `EarlyLoadingScreen.prepare`, which keeps the key's spelling, quoting and indent, a trailing comment, CRLF line endings and a byte-order mark, writes through a symbolic link rather than over it, and puts a missing key above the first table header. A quoted key used to go unrecognised and a second definition was added, which the TOML parser refuses on every start. The file is often the pack's own and tracked by a whole-file hash, so a launcher edit read as a player's edit to an update and as damage to a repair. The original is kept in `.nexira-fml-toml`, and `restore` writes it back byte for byte when the game exits or the launch fails before a spawn, unless the value was changed during the session. `restoreAll` puts back what a session left behind at the next start. `PackRuntimeSection` shows the switch for NeoForge and for Forge 1.13 to 1.16 and 1.20 onward (`appliesTo`), reads the pack's own value, and names the Wayland reason while the launcher decides.
- The Forge installer runs on the managed Java 8 whatever the game needs (`ModernInstallerResolver.FORGE_INSTALLER_JAVA`). It splits the vanilla client into a slim and an extra jar, repacks them with deflate and checks their SHA1 against hashes Forge computed with the reference zlib. On the game's JDK the repacking went through the system libz, and where that is zlib-ng (Arch, Fedora 40 onward) the same classes came out as different bytes and every Forge 1.20.1 install failed with "Processor failed, invalid outputs". Java 8 carries its own zlib, and the installer and every processor it runs are Java 8 bytecode. The game still launches on the Java its version declares, and NeoForge, which does not check those hashes, keeps running its installer on the game's Java.
- The per-session copies of a game's output that the console writes for the Logs tab are held to an age and a size. The logback channels rotate themselves, but the `game-output-*` files, one per launch, were never deleted, and a few months came to over six hundred files and half a gigabyte. `LogRetention` runs once per start off the boot path: files older than 30 days go, then the oldest until the rest fit in 300 MB, and a `.tmp` older than an hour goes too, since an interrupted log compression leaves one and nothing else removes it. A file touched in the last minute is never removed, and logback's own files and console exports are left alone.
- `initialWindowSize` raises the requested size to a floor of 800dp wide instead of 960 when the work area is smaller. On an 800x600 display the window was created wider than the screen, and where nothing maximizes it, such as a display with no window manager, its right edge stayed off screen. The resize minimum in `AppShell` is unchanged.
- `HtmlRender` lays out a description's tables as the grid they are used as. A cell was drawn as one run of text, so a picture became its alt text and a heading ran into the paragraph under it. `TableCell` lays out a cell holding blocks or images like the rest of the page and draws a plain text cell as before, with its own alignment, and a header cell stays bold either way. Column widths are resolved once per table by `tableColumns`, with the first width declared in each column winning, so a table that declares them on its header no longer splits every body row evenly, and `rowSlots` gives a short row empty slots so it still lines up. Percentages are shares of the row, scaled up when they fall short of a hundred, and pixel widths count as proportions when no cell uses a percentage. A colspan counts as that many columns, an undeclared column keeps a floor of fifteen percent, fixed columns are held to most of the row, and `declaredWidth` refuses infinite widths and caps percentages at a hundred and pixels at 4096. The column rules are drawn at the measured boundaries instead of as stretched siblings, since stretching needs the row's intrinsic height and nested tables would nest that measurement. An image with a percentage width takes that share of its column and, once loaded, its own proportions, since inside a scrolling page the loader does not scale up and a small picture at 100% sat in the middle of an empty box. Images in a run or on their own honour declared pixel sizes, a picture of 48 pixels or less sits on its line beside the text, and one that cannot be fetched or fails to load shows its alt text. The inline set gains kbd, samp and tt in monospace, sup, sub, small, big and mark. `HtmlLayoutRenderTest` renders inside a vertical scroll as every screen does, and `HtmlWidthsTest` covers the width rules.
- Mod icons are looked up once, not on every launch. An icon for a mod without its own took three lookups and none outlived the process: the jar hashed in full, the catalogue asked which version owns the hash with no cache at all, and the project asked for its icon through a thirty-minute cache kept for its download count, a network request each, per mod, on every launch. `ModIconLookups` keeps the answers through the cache layer (`mod-icon-by-hash`, `mod-icon-by-project`, `file-sha1`): a file's hash by path, size and modification time for a year, and the icon for a hash and for a project for a week, refreshed behind the one shown. A mod with no icon is remembered as having none, and a failed lookup is not kept. The Coil image cache moves from its default under the system temp dir, a tmpfs emptied on every boot on many Linux machines and shared with any program on the same library, to `cache/images` in the data dir, capped at 256 MB.
- On X11 the sideways wheel scrolls sideways instead of paging history. X11 spends buttons 4 to 7 on the wheel, so AWT hands a tilted wheel, or a touchpad swiped sideways under XWayland, to listeners as buttons 4 and 5, and the side buttons as 6 and 7, while the shell read 4 and 5 as back and forward on every toolkit. `extraButton` now navigates on 6 and 7 only when `awtOnX11`, and `resendAsSidewaysWheel` gives 4 and 5 back to the window as a wheel turn with Shift held. Other toolkits keep 4 and 5 as the side buttons.
- The editor bar in `EditorSurfaceHost` measures its labelled form and composes the icon-only one only when that is too wide, instead of switching at a window width. The threshold was right for one set of chips: on a 1366 window the labelled bar ran past the edge and the exit hint was squeezed into a staircase five rows tall.
- The severity colour rows in `ConsoleSection` pin their label at 64dp. `NxColorField` fills its row, so a label weighted to take the remainder was measured at zero and broke into one letter per line. The colour field in a highlight rule card is weighted so the bold chip after it is measured first.
- Switching pack page tabs is one fade. A tab body that fills itself in after its first frame showed as several cuts: the worlds tab drew a frame of bare wallpaper while it scanned and then its panel, and the files tab drew two empty panes before the tree. `WorldsTabPane` draws its panel from the first frame with the scan inside and fades between its states, and `PackDetailScreen` fades the body being left out quickly and the next one in after it.
- The release workflow hands its notes over as files. `build_release.yml` passed both notes from the changelog job as job outputs, which reach a step as environment variables, and the release body reached `softprops/action-gh-release` as an input, which is one too. Linux refuses to start a process with a single variable past 128 KiB, and this release's engineering log is 176 KB, so publishing would have failed after every build had finished. The changelog job now uploads `engineering.md` and `player.md` as the `release-notes` artifact, the manifest reads the player notes through `jq --rawfile`, `compose-release-body.sh` takes file paths, and the body goes to the action through `body_path`. GitHub also refuses a release body past 125000 characters, so the script measures what it composed and, when it is over, puts a link to `CHANGELOG.md` at the tag in place of the What's Changed text rather than cutting the log mid entry.
- The release's test job runs `:widget-loader:test`, `:experimental:client-boot:test` and `:examples:widget-pixelplayer:test` again. It had drifted from `tests.yml`, so the run that gates a tag checked less than a pull request does.

## [2.4.5] - 2026-09-15

### Added
- `SmrtSource` learns the `github` variant, so a pack may pin a mod to a release asset and the launcher installs it. Sixteen entries across five packs were served out of the mirror's own cache for no reason other than that there was no way to say where they came from, which put this deployment in the business of redistributing other people's builds. The variant carries `repo`, `tag`, `asset` and the `url`: a release download is public and derivable, unlike a signed CurseForge link, so `resolveUrl` hands back what the manifest says and nothing expires. `stableKey` keys such an entry by the repository alone: the tag is the version by definition and the asset name carries it by convention (`mymod-1.2.3.jar`), so either one re-keys the entry at every release, which is what the key exists to prevent. Two assets of one repository in one pack are the curator `slug`'s case rather than a cost every entry pays. `SourceBadge` and `NxColors.source` are exhaustive over the sealed class, so both were decided rather than defaulted. The token follows GitHub's monochrome palette at the shade a white label survives: `NxSourceBadge` paints the brand colour as the pill and draws the label in `Color.White`, so the near-white end of that scale put white on near-white at 1.59:1, and `#6E7781` reads 5.51:1 against the label while staying 3.02:1 off the surface.

### Fixed
- A pack launch whose roster check fails now repairs against the pinned manifest and asks again, instead of dropping the session token over files it could fetch. An instance falls behind without anyone editing it: a launcher version that could not install an entry is followed by one that can, and what it skipped is still what sits in `mods/`, which `enforceRoster` reads as the instance not being the pack. `LauncherController.catchUpWithPack` runs only after the first verdict already failed, measures against `pinnedPackVersion` rather than the mirror's latest so a launch cannot become an unasked-for update, and leaves the first verdict standing on any failure, so an unreachable mirror or an entry nobody may serve ends exactly where it ended before. `verifyAndRepair` moves onto `IPackSyncService`, since the launch path needs the operation the repair button already had.
- `TransferEngine.probe` demotes to streaming when a host refuses the ranged request with a status that will not change, instead of reading it as the object being absent. The probe asks `bytes=0-0` to learn the length and prove ranges in one request; CurseForge's `edge.forgecdn.net` answers that with 404 while the same URL with no Range answers 302 to a host that serves the file and advertises `accept-ranges: bytes` there. Only transfers above `DEFAULT_PARALLEL_THRESHOLD` probe at all, so a pack's small mods streamed fine and a 24 MiB library pinned on CurseForge could not be installed, which left the instance failing `enforceRoster` over one file and launching without a token. A retryable status is still retried rather than costing the rest of a large transfer its blocks.
- `SmrtSyncService.writeRoster` and `writeSourceMarker` publish through `AtomicFiles` instead of a truncating `writeText`. The roster is the delete list `enforceRoster` applies on the next launch, and `readRoster` returns a `Set<String>` with no count and no digest, so a prefix left by a crash mid-write is indistinguishable from a shorter pack and the sweep removes every mod whose name did not survive. The failure this trades for is keeping the previous roster, which names a build that was on disk.
- `PackSnapshotService.capture` takes the instance's own two files, `.nexira-mods` and `.nexira-sync-source`, alongside the manifest paths. Neither is a manifest path, so the scan never listed them and `restore` never put them back: a rollback left the roster naming the build it rolled back from, which on an instance with no registry baseline outranks nothing and takes the restored build's mods out at the next launch. The capture is hardlinked, so this only holds because the roster is now published by replacing the file rather than truncating it in place, and the two changes are one.
- `SmrtSyncService.planMod` and the update path keep the result of the stale-variant delete instead of discarding it, and `sweepStale` asks again once the transfers are done. During an update of a mod the player disabled, that stale variant is the ACTIVE jar: a holder outliving `fileOpRetry` left it there, the new copy landed as `.disabled` beside it, `relabel` then declined because both names existed, and the roster names both by design so `pruneForeignEntries` took neither. What survives the second attempt is reported through the log and `ActionRing` in the toggle path's own words, and only for the active variant, since a leftover `.disabled` is a file no loader opens.
- `LauncherController.setOptionalMods` re-reads the instance before writing rather than persisting the record the caller captured. An apply committing between the Content tab's render and the flip moves `pinnedPackVersion`, `installedManifest` and `cachedManifest`, and writing the captured copy whole restored all three to the pre-apply build, so a checkbox undid an update. The two other writers in that file already re-read.
- `PackOperationPhase.Repaired` carries the failed entries, and `PackDataSection` stops dropping `RepairReport.failed` on the floor. A repair that could not fetch half the pack rendered through the same `colors.success` line as one that found nothing wrong, while the roster was correctly withheld and the next launch went without a token. Both surfaces that render the phase name the count and the first few files, via `shortNameList`, because placing the file by hand is the answer to most of them and that needs a filename.

## [2.4.4] - 2026-09-15

### Fixed
- `SmrtSource` learns the `curseforge` variant, so a pack that pins mods there installs them. The sealed class knew three types and folded anything else to `Unknown`, which `SmrtSyncService.plan` skips by design; the mirror gained a CurseForge pin and five packs moved most of their mods onto it, so between four and sixty-one mods per pack were left out of every install with nothing logged but a forward-compat warning. What reached the player depended on the instance: where the pack keeps a filename across builds the previous build's bytes stayed on disk, so `enforceRoster` read the entry as mismatched and the launch got no token, and on a fresh install the file was simply absent, which the roster check does not test for and the server refuses the join over. The variant carries `project_id`, `file_id` and the `url` the mirror resolved at build time: turning a file id into a link needs the mirror's API key, so `resolveUrl` reads the manifest and the launcher never talks to CurseForge. A `url` is absent exactly when the project's author has disabled third-party distribution, and that entry is skipped on its own branch with its own message, because in a log it is indistinguishable from an unsupported type and only one of the two is fixed by updating. `SourceBadge` and `NxColors.source` are exhaustive over the sealed class, so both were decided rather than defaulted, and CurseForge gets its own brand colour in each theme.
- `SmrtSyncService.plan` answers `Planned` instead of a nullable `Transfer`, so a repair stops calling a pack whole while a mod is missing from it. `Fetch`, `UpToDate` and `Unfetchable` were two of them collapsed into one `null`: everything outside the suspect list counted as `intact`, so `verifyAndRepair` returned a clean report for an entry it had just decided nothing could obtain. `Unfetchable` now carries the reason and lands in `RepairReport.failed` beside a transfer that could not finish, which is the same fact about the instance. The order inside `plan` is reversed with it: `isUpToDate` is asked before anything about the source, so a mod the player installed by hand, which is what the log for a withheld entry tells them to do, is recognised rather than warned about and reported missing. `sync` and `applyUpdate` collect through a shared `PlanSink` and record the shortfall in `ActionRing`, because an install that quietly places less than the pack describes otherwise surfaces as the loader or the server refusing it.
- `SmrtSourceLenientSerializer` and `SmrtAuthLenientSerializer` fold a malformed payload the way their KDoc always said they did. Both matched the discriminator and then called `decodeFromJsonElement` unguarded, so a known `type` carrying an unexpected shape threw out of the whole `SmrtPackManifest` decode: one entry the mirror and the launcher disagree about cost the pack every other entry, which is the exact failure the unknown-type branch exists to prevent. `curseforge` makes it likelier by adding two numeric fields where the other variants carry strings.
- `SmrtModEntry.stableKey` reads a CurseForge `project_id` after a Modrinth one, so an optional mod pinned there keeps its on/off state across a pack-version bump instead of falling back to a filename carrying the mod version. `OptionalContentRules.enabledState` already matches a persisted filename key as a fallback, and `ModReconciler` already guards a re-keyed identity by path, so the change does not orphan what is stored.
- `ModIconResolver` resolves a non-Modrinth entry through the manifest's own sha1, the hash lookup `resolveByFile` uses, so a mod published on CurseForge and on Modrinth alike keeps its icon. Asking CurseForge would need a CurseForge key, which only the mirror holds; Modrinth answers hash-to-project for anyone. One cache keyed by hash serves both entry points, nulls included.

## [2.4.3] - 2026-09-12

### Fixed
- The content guard is aimed at what the loader reads rather than at everything under `mods/`. Four mods put an archive there during one launch and each had needed a rule of its own: a manifest `ContainedDeps`, a library unpacked into `mods/ic2/`, jars a dependency loader moved into `mods/<mcversion>/`, and a texture cache written into `mods/carpentersblocks/`. Forge's `Loader` calls `findModDirMods` on `mods/` and, when it exists, on `mods/<mcversion>/`, and neither read recurses, so an archive anywhere else is a mod's own storage and is left alone. The rule also never looked at the other shape found in those two directories. A directory is a candidate in its own right, read as an unpacked mod through `ContainerType.DIR`, and `.class` is not a name the sweep treats as loadable, so a mod dropped in unpacked was the one shape that ran unquestioned while a jar buried where nothing reads was swept diligently. A directory the loader reads is now checked for a mod, by its metadata or by the classes it carries, and reported rather than deleted, because removing a tree the launcher did not create is a different act from dropping a stray jar. The instance is held unverified either way. `ModArchives.carriedArchiveNames` goes with the per-mod rules it was written for. The guarantee narrows from no archive anywhere under `mods/` that the pack did not ship, to nothing the loader will run that the pack did not ship.
- The adaptive heap stops learning from launches that failed. `HeapDeriver` already rejected a record with no GC and no peak, but an aborted launch has both, and those arrive in runs, so a window fills with them and the good samples are evicted: an instance was found at 1024 MB on three samples, a crash at mod init and two launches the content guard ended at 1.5 and 1.3 seconds. A session now has to have lasted a minute to count as a measurement. The live set is the quieter half. It is only established when a major collection settles it, so an instance roomy enough never to fill reports none at all and leaves the peak as the only reading, and peak is a floor under the demand rather than a measure of it. Taking it as the answer shrank the heap every session, and each smaller heap made the next reading look better justified. Without one reliable live set in the window the heap may now rise and not fall.

## [2.4.2] - 2026-09-12

### Fixed
- The two undeclared ways a mod puts a jar beside the mods, each of which ended the session as tampering. `ContainedDeps` is the declared route and the guard already knew it, but on 1.7.10 both of the others turned up in one launch. IndustrialCraft 2 keeps `lib/EJML-core-0.26.jar` packed inside itself and lays it out under `mods/ic2/`, naming neither the file nor the directory anywhere a manifest reader would look, so `ModArchives.carriedArchiveNames` reads the carried names out of the jar rather than out of a list written here. CodeChickenCore's dependency loader does the reverse and moves jars the pack already shipped into `mods/<mcversion>/`, leaving the pack's own mods where the pack never put them. `SmrtSyncService.isRelocatedPackMod` recognises those by the digest the pack declared rather than by the name, so a move is allowed and a substitution under a familiar name still is not. The directory stays unexempted because the loader reads it, and an instance with no baseline has no digests to match, so it keeps the stricter reading.

## [2.4.1] - 2026-09-12

### Added
- Japanese joins the interface languages, and a bundled face that can draw it. `JapaneseStrings` implements every member of `AppStrings` with one plural form and no count selector, and `AppLocale.JAPANESE` marks it experimental in the picker. The face is the harder half: Compose picks one face per family by weight and style and never by what it contains, so a family cannot borrow coverage from a sibling and a missing glyph leaves the bundle for whatever the host has. Roboto Flex ships subset to Latin, Cyrillic and Greek, so the answer is a second bundled family chosen per string. `needsCjkFace` in `CjkText` answers from `CJK_ONLY_RUNS`, which `tools/fonts/regenerate.py` reads out of the two shipped binaries rather than out of the sources they were subset from. The CJK face is the pan-CJK JP face subset by an explicit range policy: one shared codepoint map serves every regional variant and they differ only in which glyph a codepoint maps to, so this covers the script while drawing Han in Japanese forms. Which face draws the interface itself is decided by asking the active locale's own strings, not by matching a list of language tags, so the next locale that needs this is picked up without editing a list.
- `CHANGELOG_JA.md`, the player notes in Japanese, starting at this release. `UpdateService.fetchLocalizedNotes` builds the filename from the locale's own tag, so a shipped locale whose file is missing is an error nothing reports: the reader is handed the English copy CI froze into the release manifest instead. The file lands with the locale rather than after it for that reason, and its version headers are byte-identical to the English ones, because `extractVersionSection` selects a section by `## [version]` and by nothing else.
- Three new player kinds in `client-ui/widgets/sample/players`, drawn from the concept sheet rather than invented at the keyboard. `CoverPlayerWidget` leads with the artwork, `SeededPlayerWidget` takes the card's body colour from the cover through the same quantizer the wallpaper palette uses, and `ReadoutPlayerWidget` shows no picture at all and makes the elapsed time the hero, in the mono family so the digits hold still under a clock that ticks five times a second. `TimelinePlayerWidget` has no progress bar because the filled part of the card is the position, and the plane takes the drag. Each measures its own width through `BoxWithConstraints` and drops its parts in a fixed order, because a widget's width is the slot's call and a row of fixed measurements breaks below the width it was drawn at.
- A queue under the player. `AudioPlayer` holds `queue: List<Path>` and `queueIndex` instead of one file, `currentFile` is derived so the two cannot part, and the poll loop's Ended branch advances rather than reopening the same file. The picker takes several files with no second verb beside it. `nextQueueIndex`, `previousQueueIndex` and `removeFromQueue` are pure functions beside `mapPlaybackState` and for the same reason: the natives are not on the test path, so the edges have to be answerable without one.
- Loudness that survives a restart, in `SettingsData.audioVolume`, seeded into `AudioPlayer` and written back through a debounce so a drag across the track is one file write and not one per pointer frame.
- Two more channels behind the news rail. `NewsChannelPolicy` travels with the feed rather than being set at the call site, so a surface cannot be configured into opening a channel that should not be opened. `CuratedNewsFeed` ships a pool of lines in the jar and needs nothing configured, overridable at `<dataDir>/news-curated.txt` and re-read per open. `SyndicationNewsFeed` reads any RSS or Atom address named in settings, over jsoup's XML parser, bounded, http and https only. `SyndicationParser` drops the entry's link and its images deliberately: nothing downstream can then open a page nobody vetted, and no remote asset is fetched to render a row.
- `MemoryReport` and the console commands that print it. Heap ceiling, collector thread counts and region size all come from the host's RAM and cores, so a footprint measured on one machine says little about anyone else's. The report reads the management beans, `/proc/self/smaps` and the native memory tracker from inside the process.
